**Q102. `compare` measures side A's open ranges in the wrong document; Ferrum keeps cm6's code until the delta, and F5046 is listed.** At `rangeset.ts:889` cm6 compares `a.openEnd(end)`, where `end` is a position of the new document and `a.activeTo` are positions of the old one. I replayed chunk `rangeset` on cm6 with `a.openEnd(end - dPos)` there. Nine fixtures change, all in their `bound` records and nothing else. cm6 misses `boundChange(6)` in F5009 and `boundChange(55)` in F5019, and makes calls the corrected code does not make in six others (more than a hundred in F5081). The view turns each call into a range to redraw (`docview.ts:637`, `:650`). So cm6 sometimes fails to redraw a changed split and sometimes asks for redraws of nothing. That is a defect of cm6 against its own documentation of `boundChange` (`rangeset.ts:70-73`) and of the comparator's positions (`:64-66`).

D1 turns this into a question of evidence as well as of correctness. In F5046 the gap's offset is -1 in code units and +1 in bytes, so the wrong comparison lands elsewhere, and Ferrum, running cm6's algorithm exactly, makes a call cm6 did not (the implementer's probe with `"x"` in place of U+2028 gave cm6's calls). Until the comparison is corrected, the `bound` calls of a `compare` after a non-ASCII edit are neither cm6's nor right, and no conversion recovers cm6's.

So: (1) the correction is `state.imp.013`, *scheduled* for the improvement delta of `ferrum-state`, with the one-line patch as its reference, which I ran. Once it is made, the calls no longer depend on the unit, and F5046 is compared again (that it then equals its record is a deduction, not run). (2) Until then, `state.cm6.188.d2` (class `reproduce`) keeps cm6's code and lists F5046. The core already runs a listed fixture, marks it `none`, and fails the run if it ever equals its record, so the row needs no replayer work.
- *Lost:* a condition the replayer detects (any `compare` with a `boundChange` comparator whose gap offsets differ between the units). Applied to the comparator's whole list, it would leave out the `range` and `point` records of 25 non-ASCII fixtures to cover one that differs. Applied to the `bound` records alone, it needs a projection of lists that the core does not have, built for a rule the delta then removes. *Lost:* make the correction now. That builds the delta's reference machinery in the middle of U3, and nine ASCII fixtures would need patched values before the floor is shown. That is the "second novelty" D16's timing exists to avoid. *Lost:* keep the fixture as a question. It blocks the unit on a case whose cause is known.
- *For conformance:* G5's fresh seeds will produce such fixtures at about the rate seen here (1 of 25 non-ASCII `compare` fixtures with bounds). The row's text names the signature ("differs only in `bound` records of a `compare` whose gap offsets differ between the units"), and the conformance plan, which I write at the hand-off, classifies them by it.

**Q103. `state.SpanIterator.point.B2` is the Rust form: `state.cm6.211.d1`, class `form`.** The behavior is that cm6 passes the same array object to `point` when no range is active. A callee in Rust gets a slice borrowed for the call and cannot keep it, so neither the sharing nor a later mutation can be observed. What the callee is given is compared through `spans`. It is Q96's case (`state.cm6.171.d1`): a property of JavaScript objects with no form for a value.

**Q104. A set built by `set.of` and given to `compare` as a new set is measured on the document `changes` produces (rule 12).** `raw/positions.jsonl` (`compare`) names only sets that come from `set.map`. cm6's comparator contract is that new sets are in new-document positions, however they were built, and the four astral fixtures (F6040, F6042, F6044, F6046) are written that way. So for that call the replayer builds the set through the new document (`RangeSet::of` on the same ranges, positions converted). A set already bound to a document that converts differently has no counterpart there, reason `char`. I checked the expected outcome before deciding: cm6 run on the four inputs with each inserted string replaced by ASCII of its UTF-8 length, which is what Ferrum computes in bytes, gives exactly the records once converted back. F6043 and F6047, where U+2028 is the line separator and counts one in both units, already pass.

**The seven cases the code step listed with no behavior row.** For each, I asked whether a caller can observe it and whether the fixtures already pin it. The second is a run: the case's code changed in the harness's copy of cm6, and chunk `rangeset` replayed.

| Case (`rangeset.ts`) | Observable | Run on cm6 with it changed | Decision |
|---|---|---|---|
| `:284` a one-range chunk is never reused whole by `update` | yes: chunk layout and identity, which `dump` and `compare`'s sharing show | reusing one-range chunks changes 9 fixtures (F0106, F0107, seven of `state.cm6.181`) | no new fixture; step 3 corrects `state.RangeSet.update.B6`, which omits it |
| `:543` `addChunk` reads the first value only when the positions tie | no: one O(1) read that decides no result | reading it always changes no fixture | no row |
| `:606-620` a forward step never moves a cursor back | yes, as the absence of repeated ranges that every `spans` and `compare` behavior states | without the guard cm6 runs out of memory on the chunk | no row |
| `:702-706`, `:914-922` the `TypeError`s at `C.Far` | unreachable inside `RangeSet`'s domain (`state.cm6.187.d1`) | not run | no row |
| `:777-781` `SpanCursor.forward` removes ended ranges first | no: what it removes, `next` removes or `activeForPoint` and `openEnd` leave out, and it does not touch `trackOpen` (a deduction from the code) | removing it changes no fixture | no row |
| `:162-172` where `Chunk.map`'s backward scan stops | yes: the layer a mapped point lands in | a scan that does not stop changes F5080 | no new fixture; step 3 corrects `state.RangeSet.map.B6`, which states the stop inverted ("while the earlier range does not start after it"; cm6 moves back only past ranges that start at or after the point) |

So step 3 of U3 writes no new row and no fixture. It corrects two rows, three statements in all, which the specification's own verification passed with a wrong or incomplete text. Each corrected row carries `added`, so that conformance reads it again: `specification.md` §4 now says `added` marks a corrected row too. The fixtures were right throughout, because they come from cm6. What was wrong is the text a reader of the specification relies on, and the maintainer's intent is that something known to be wrong is fixed when it is found. The run is now how the step is settled (`plan.md` step 3). It replaced a reading that, for `:543` and `:777`, could only have argued.

**Native mechanisms.** I read each, and only "Signed offsets in usize" needs a change (Q100). Values cloned into `SpanCursor`'s lists is the `T: Clone` of `design.md` §3, and costs one clone of a value per range when it becomes active, where cm6 stores a reference.

**What it requires, in this order.**
1. *The U3 implementer, resumed* (budget 40,000): `crate::rangeset::Chunk`'s `from` and `to`, and the builder's arrays, hold `isize`; `store` and `rel` go; `__test::Chunk::from` and `to` return `&[isize]`, their doc comments saying the offsets are negative for a range stored with `from > to` and for one added after it (`state.cm6.201.d1`, `state.RangeSetBuilder.add.B6`); the item "Signed offsets in usize" of `implementation.md` ("Native mechanisms", unit rangeset) is rewritten to what the code then does. Checks: `cargo build`, `cargo clippy`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and the ported and written test targets of the unit. The target `replay` does not compile until item 2, and the report says so. The orchestrator runs nothing else before item 2.
2. *The U3 test-writer, a round* (budget 80,000, counted as a round of "A check that is wrong"): the replayer reads `__test::Chunk::from` and `to` as `&[isize]` (`chunk_ranges` in `replay/rangeset/values.rs`, `dump` in `replay/rangeset/sets.rs`, and every other reader); `maxPoint` conversion follows rule 11 with the scope widened to the set; `compare` follows rule 12 (a `set.of` set given as new is built through the document `changes` produces; a set bound to another document that converts differently is no counterpart, reason `char`), with a literal test of rule 12 in the chunk's harness tests. Each file listed in `implementation.md` "Changes to checks after their code", with the rule it restores (`design.md` §2 for the reader, `plan.md` "Positions" rules 11 and 12). Check: `python3 tools/impl_check.py ferrum-state code rangeset --since 126dad7` passes whole. Expected there: F0087, F0088 and F0235 compared; F5017 and F6040 to F6046 `equal`; F5046 `none` under `state.cm6.188.d2`; `state.SpanIterator.point.B2` accepted under `state.cm6.211.d1`; the three behaviors of Q100 named by a check that runs. A fixture that does not end that way goes through `tools/trace_diff.py` and comes back as a question.
3. *Step 3 of U3, one `spec-auditor`* (budget 60,000, `high`), after item 2 passes. It corrects, from cm6's code and not from this entry's wording, `state.RangeSet.map.B6`: where the backward scan stops (`rangeset.ts:164`, `:170`; F5080 shows it), and that the chunk's `maxPoint` counts every mapped point before the keep-or-spill decision (`:153`, `:155`; F0133, F0135, F0137). It also corrects `state.RangeSet.update.B6`: a chunk is reused whole only when it holds two ranges or more, because after the only range of a one-range chunk is read the cursor is already at the next chunk with `rangeIndex` 0 (`:284`, `:646-652`), so its range is added again (F0106, F0107 show it). Each corrected row gets `"added": "implementation rangeset"`. No fixture is added. If a cited fixture does not show its statement, the auditor reports it and adds none. Check: `python3 tools/spec_check.py ferrum-state` ends with "checks passed". Then one call of mine reads the two rows, and the orchestrator runs `code rangeset --since 126dad7` again, and U3 is done.

**Confidence.** High on Q100: it is a type matching what it holds, and the rule of `design.md` §2. High on Q101 and on the seven cases: each rests on a run of cm6. The judgments of observability for `:543` and `:777` are deductions, medium-high. High that cm6's `:889` is wrong, by run. Medium-high on keeping it until the delta with F5046 listed: it is right on evidence and timing, and its cost lands on G5, where I have named it. High on Q103 and Q104, Q104 with its outcome checked on cm6 in bytes.

**Reopens.** A fixture that fails under rule 11 on a point the set no longer holds. More than one fixture in conformance's fresh seeds differing outside `bound` records in a `compare` after a non-ASCII edit (then `:889` is not the only cause). A consumer in `ferrum-view` that relies on cm6's extra `boundChange` calls (`state.imp.013`). A listed case judged unobservable that a later fixture or the oracle shows observable.

**Escapes** (ledger): `state.RangeSet.map.B6` states the scan's stop inverted and the chunk's `maxPoint` wrongly, and `state.RangeSet.update.B6` omits one-range chunks. All three were made at S2 and found here, low, because the fixtures were right. My rule 11 at Q99 named a scope that F5017 shows too narrow, low.

## 2026-11-19 · Step 3 of U3 read: the two corrected rows stand; their new fixtures must name them back

*Decided by the consultant, the reading item 3 of "Q100 to Q104" asks for, of step 3 of U3 (commit bcae032). Binding text: `plan.md` "Each unit" step 3 (a fixture a corrected row comes to cite names the behavior back); `tools/spec_check.py` (the check of that link, both ways) and `tools/README.md`. Ledger: E33, one escape, partial results of E31 and E32.*

**Question.** Do the corrected rows `state.RangeSet.map.B6` and `state.RangeSet.update.B6` state what cm6 does, and is anything else needed before U3 is done and U4 starts?

**What I read and ran.** The two rows before and after (`git show bcae032`); `mirrors/cm6/state/src/rangeset.ts:133-177` (`Chunk.map`), `:271-300` (`RangeSet.update`), `:303-332` (`RangeSet.map`), `:524-538` (`addInner`), `:542-553` (`addChunk`), `:600-656` (`LayerCursor`); the fixtures each row cites for a statement (F0106, F0107, F0133, F0135, F0137, F5080, with their inputs, values and outputs), traced by hand through that code. Then the join of every behavior row's `fixtures` with every fixture's `behaviors` over the whole specification, and a regeneration of chunk `rangeset` in a scratch copy with the change below.

**The rows stand.** Every statement holds against the code and the fixture it cites:
- `map.B6`: append when `(newFrom - basePos || startSide - baseSide) >= 0` (`:155`), the base starting at the last kept chunk's end and side or -1/-1 (`:320-322`); the backward scan inserts after a kept range it starts at or after (`:165`), stops at one that starts before it, comparing that range's `startSide` with its own `endSide` (`:170`), and moves back otherwise; an insertion leaves the base (only `:159-160` set it); the chunk starts at the first mapped range even when that one spills (`:152`); `maxPoint` is counted at `:153`, before `:155`. F0133 and F0135 give a chunk holding only `[0,3]` with `maxPoint` 0 from a spilled empty point; F0137 gives layer 0 `maxPoint` 0 and the mapped chunk at 250 with its range at offset 10; in F5080 the range from 5 maps to 2, fails the append against base 2/300000001, and stops at the kept range at 2, so it lands in layer 1 at 2, which is the record. The added statement "an insertion does not move the base", which no cited fixture isolates, is read directly off the code.
- `update.B6`: after reading the only range of a one-range chunk, `setRangeIndex` has moved the cursor to the next chunk with `rangeIndex` 0 (`:645-652`), so `cur.rangeIndex == 1` (`:284`) fails and the range goes through `addInner`. F0106 and F0107 give exactly the layouts the row states, and in F0107 `[25,26]` spills because `addInner` refuses a start before the last end (`:525`, `:528`).

**What is not right: four links written one way.** The corrected rows list F0106, F0107 (`update.B6`), F0137 and F5080 (`map.B6`), and those fixtures do not name the behaviors back. Across the 952 rows and 4,965 fixtures of the specification these are the only four such links; in both directions every other link is written twice. It matters because the two sides are read by different readers: the replayer and `tools/impl_check.py` report per behavior through the fixture's `behaviors` (`impl_check.py:492`, `:669`; `specification.md` §5 "Behaviors without a cm6 test"), and the reader of a row, conformance's reading of rows with `added` among them, goes through the row's `fixtures`. So F5080, the only fixture that pins where the scan stops, counted for `map.B6` in the row and not in any per-behavior report. No gate missed anything: every fixture is compared whatever it names, and a wrong stop fails F5080 visibly. But conformance's measure of E5 ("no behavior whose replayed fixtures fail to assert its stated result") would read `map.B6` without its one decisive fixture, and a known inconsistency in the specification is fixed when found, not carried.

The cause is mine, not the auditor's: item 3 of "Q100 to Q104" said "no fixture is added", and the card turned that into "no fixture, generator or op", which forbade the one edit that keeps the link whole. A fixture's `behaviors` come from its generator, and `spec_check` requires the file to be what the generators write, so the link is made there.

**Decision.**
1. The two rows stand as committed.
2. The links are made both ways, by the orchestrator, without an agent (the edits are fixed below; I ran them in a scratch copy: the five generators of chunk `rangeset` regenerate 394 fixtures, of which 84 differ from the committed file, in `behaviors` only, and no input or output changes):
   - `oracle/cm6/rangeset/fixtures.ts`, the loop that writes F0106 and F0107: `[...B("RangeSet.update", 4), ...B("RangeSet.update", 6), ...B("RangeSetUpdate.add", 2)]`;
   - same file, the fixture of the cross-chunk spill (F0137): `[...B("RangeSet.map", 6), ...B("RangeSet.map", 9)]`;
   - `oracle/cm6/rangeset/random.ts`, the list every random case gets (F5001 to F5081): `"state.RangeSet.map.B6"` after `"state.RangeSet.map.B3"`. Every random case maps its set and re-lays the chunks it touches, which is why `map.B3` is already on all 81; F5080 alone cannot be singled out without a condition on the case number in the generator, which would say less than the truth;
   - `raw/behaviors.jsonl`, row `state.RangeSet.map.B6`: `fixtures` gains F5001 to F5081 (F5080 is there already), so that the row lists every fixture that names it.
3. `tools/spec_check.py` fails on a link written one way, in either direction. Seen on the tree at 1a595dd: it reports exactly the four links, and the other direction is empty. It is a join, at no tokens, of what a reading found here at the cost of a consultant call.
4. `plan.md` step 3 says that a fixture a corrected row comes to cite names the behavior back, through its generator.

**Alternatives and why each lost.**
- *Leave the four links one way.* Nothing fails now, but the per-behavior reports and the row disagree about what shows `map.B6`, and the reading that conformance owes the rows with `added` would start from the wrong side for one of them.
- *Drop F0106, F0107, F0137, F5080 from the rows and keep them only in the text.* Symmetric, but the one fixture that pins the scan's stop would then show it to no tool.
- *Edit `fixtures/rangeset.jsonl` by hand.* `spec_check` regenerates the file and fails on any byte the generators do not write, and the next regeneration would undo it.
- *A spec-auditor for the edits.* Each edit is fixed above and the run shows its whole effect; an agent would add tokens and nothing a reading could find.

**What each later step does.**
1. *The orchestrator:* the four edits of decision item 2; the five generators of chunk `rangeset` in the order of `oracle/cm6/order.json` (`npx --yes tsx --tsconfig .harness/state/tsconfig.json docs/parity/ferrum-state/oracle/cm6/rangeset/<file>`); a comparison of the new `fixtures/rangeset.jsonl` with the committed one that must find 84 fixtures differing in `behaviors` only (F0106, F0107, F0137, F5001 to F5081) and nothing else; `python3 tools/spec_check.py ferrum-state` ending with "checks passed" (until then it fails on the four links, which is the check doing its work). One commit with the two generators, the fixture file, the row and the regenerated `raw/fixture-ops.jsonl`.
2. *Then* `python3 tools/impl_check.py ferrum-state code rangeset --since 126dad7` as item 3 of "Q100 to Q104" already asks. Expected: as at 7a742fe (0 mismatch); the per-behavior counts of `map.B6` and `update.B6` grow by the fixtures they gained, all `equal` except F5046, which stays `none` under `state.cm6.188.d2`. Anything else goes through `tools/trace_diff.py` and comes back as a question. Then U3 is done.
3. *U4* starts after that run, as `plan.md` and the progress file say: U4a checks of chunk `facet` (`checks state --chunk facet`, 280,000) with `--since` the commit that then holds the checks. Nothing of this entry reaches U4's cards, except that a step 3 of U4 that corrects a row follows `plan.md` step 3 as now written.

**Confidence.** High that both rows are right: each statement was traced through the code and checked against the fixture it cites, and the auditor's runs on changed copies of cm6 agree. High on making the links both ways and on the check: the join was run on the whole specification and the regeneration on a scratch copy. Medium-high on putting `map.B6` on all 81 random cases rather than on F5080 alone, for the reason given in item 2.

**Reopens.** A random case whose map touches no chunk being read in conformance as evidence of `map.B6` (then the generator gives `map.B6` only to cases whose change touches a chunk); the symmetry check failing on a relation that is meant to be one way (none exists today).

## 2026-11-19 · Q105 to Q110: what the checks of chunk `facet` compare

*Decided by the consultant, after the U4a checks (commits c777c75, 1e80420). Binding text: `raw/decisions.jsonl` `state.cm6.134.d2`; `raw/replay.jsonl` rows `combineConfig.call`, `combineConfig.entries` and `prec.keys` of chunk `facet`; `design.md` §3 (the `Extension` row) and §10 ("No counterpart" case 1, "A value a callback returns"); `.claude/rules/decisions.md` D12 (its record: `docs/decisions/foundation.md`, same date). Closes Q105 to Q110.*

**The class behind four of the six.** Q105, Q107, Q108 and Q109 are one question asked four times: the replayer met a JavaScript value or result that the skeleton's types do not name, and filed it under the reason `type` (D12). That reason is read at the approval of `fixtures/no-counterpart.jsonl` against D12 ("an engine artifact no caller relies on"), and none of the four is one. So each needed either a form in Ferrum or another reason that its reader can check. Q106 is the one that touches what Ferrum does; Q110 is a real D12 case.

**Q105. `{extension: e}` is the conversion of `e`.** cm6 takes any object with an `extension` property as an extension (`mirrors/cm6/state/src/facet.ts:549-552`, `flatten`); it is how `LanguageSupport` and `ViewPlugin` are extensions in the packages above. In Ferrum such a type implements `From<T> for Extension`, giving the conversion of its content, with the content's identity (`design.md` §3 already says an `Extension` has the identity of what it was converted from). What could differ is `flatten`'s `seen` map, which keys the wrapper object itself. I derived that it cannot: whenever cm6 records a wrapper at a precedence it walks the content at that precedence, so the content's recorded precedence is never weaker than the wrapper's, and a repeated wrapper is skipped exactly when its content would be. Then I ran it: every chunk replayed on the harness's cm6 with such objects unwrapped before `seen` (5 chunks, 4,966 fixture runs): no fixture changed. The one remaining difference is identity: two wrappers around one content are one extension in Ferrum and two objects in cm6, which a caller sees only by comparing what `Compartment::get` returns; none of the 45 fixtures that use a wrapper runs an identity op or `compartment.get`. So the 45 fixtures are compared: the replayer gives Ferrum the content's `Extension`; a wrapper whose `extension` is falsy or itself stays excluded by type (cm6's "Unrecognized extension value", which `design.md` §3 says Ferrum has no case for). *Lost:* keeping them out by type (they are cm6's ordinary extension protocol, not an artifact, and `state.Extension.B2` would have no compared fixture); a public `Extension::wrap` with its own identity (API cm6 does not have, for a difference no fixture shows). The identity difference is written into the `Extension` row so that `ferrum-view` and `ferrum-language`, which define such types, meet it in their specification.

**Q106. The field a conflict names follows the caller's merge order.** cm6 merges config by config and names the first conflict in that order (`mirrors/cm6/state/src/config.ts:14-19`). `combine_config` merges one field (`state.cm6.134.d1`), so the loop over fields belongs to the config facet that calls it, in its struct's field order. I computed the fixtures this changes instead of trusting the count: chunk `facet` replayed on cm6 with `combineConfig`'s two loops swapped (keys in first-appearance order) differs in exactly the eight the test-writer named, F5125, F5127, F5128, F5131, F5144, F5157, F5178, F5183; the other four chunks change in none. All eight are random cases that conflict in two fields at once. Decision: keep the per-field form, and record the consequence as `state.cm6.134.d2` (class `form`, no condition, the eight listed: each is run, must differ from its record, and is not compared). What stays cm6's: whether a merge panics, the message's form, the call it panics in, and every result of a merge that does not panic (fields merge independently). What differs: which of several conflicting fields the message names, and which `combine` functions run before it, visible only to a function with side effects. Who sees it: a developer whose configuration conflicts in two fields at once, in a panic message that is true either way and points at a real conflict. *Lost:* a step form (`combine_config` called per config and field, so that callers fold config-major): every config facet of the four crates (history, bracket matching, folding, search, lint, gutters among them) would write a fold over a struct of accumulators instead of one call per field, to choose which of two true messages a programming error prints; a replayer that orders the calls to match each fixture's record (the replayer would then compute cm6's answer instead of Ferrum's). D11 is not stretched: the input of `combine_config` is one field, for which it gives cm6's result; the difference is in code a caller writes. The U4 implementer adds a `/// cm6 deviation:` line to `combine_config` citing the row, so that the crates above find it.

**Q107. A merge with no key shows nothing of Ferrum.** F0159 merges no config and no default: its result `{}` is computed by the replayer without calling Ferrum, and, an empty object being a compared position, it would read `equal`. The test-writer was right not to count it, but `type` says the input cannot be written, and it can (a config struct with no fields). Decision: rows of `raw/replay.jsonl` for `combineConfig.entries` and `combineConfig.call`, `part`, naming "the result of a merge in which no config and no default has a key", so the reason is `op` and its reader checks it against the row. `state.combineConfig.B10` is not left without a fixture, as the question says: F0160 (no config, two defaults) calls Ferrum per default and is compared.

**Q108. `Object.keys(Prec)` is a `none` op.** It reads the own keys of a JavaScript object of functions; `Prec` is an uninhabited enum (N16), and that its five functions exist is what `impl_check skeleton` counts. The precedent is `charCategory.keys` and `mapmode.values` (Q86): a row `none`. The rest of F0119 is compared.

**Q109. A value cm6 reads only by a rule of the language is given as what the rule makes of it.** D12 and Q91 named only a `null` cm6 treats as absent. The replayer met three more shapes of the same thing and filed the truthy half under `type` while it already gave the falsy half as the absent value (`scope.rs` `falsy_value`): what a `compare` returns (27 uses), `static: 1`, `require` other than a boolean or `undefined`. I read every site: what a `compare` or `compareInput` returns is read only in `if`, `!`, `||` and `?:` (`facet.ts:166`, `:180`, `:198`, `:238`, `:250`, `:333`, `:498`); `static` only in `!!config.static` (`:74`); `require` only in `if (require)` after its default for `undefined` (`mirrors/cm6/state/src/state.ts:68-71`); `combine`, `compareInput`, `compare`, `provide` and `get` are tested and replaced by their default when falsy (`facet.ts:71-73`, `:109`, `:312-313`), and a `toJSON` or `fromJSON` that is not a function throws on its call the same `TypeError` as an absent one (`state.ts:213`, `:229`). None of these results is an engine artifact: the language defines truthiness, JavaScript callers write `compare: (a, b) => a && b && …` and rely on it, and a Rust caller writes the `bool`. D12 now says so for every such value: treated exactly as absent, it is `None`; read only for truthiness, it is the test's `bool`; the parameter's default first, for `undefined` only; any other read keeps it excluded. A wrong conversion cannot pass silently: the expected outputs are cm6's, so a site where cm6 did something else fails its fixture. *Lost:* keeping the truthy half excluded (an inconsistent rule, and fixtures compared nowhere); widening Ferrum's signatures to take such values (API for JavaScript's sake, which D12 forbids).

**Q110. A `computeN` getter returning a Set, a Map or a generator stays excluded by type.** cm6 declares `readonly Input[]`; it spreads the result with the iteration protocol but compares it through `.length` (`facet.ts:196-200`), which these objects lack, so a changed value is taken as unchanged and a generator is spent after one use (`state.Facet.computeN.B8`). That is D12's case and D11's class (c): reading `undefined` gives the result, and no caller relies on losing its changes. Ferrum's getter returns a `Vec`, whose length is real, so the defect cannot be written; there is no choice to register as an improvement. `B8` has four fixtures (F7109 to F7112), all excluded by type, which `impl_check` sorts as such.

**What each later step does.**
1. *A round of the U4a `test-writer`* (60,000, "A check that is wrong" budget; committed alone and listed under "Changes to checks after their code" with the rule each change restores) on `crates/ferrum-state/tests/replay/facet/`: Q105, a wrapper given where an extension is expected becomes its content's `Extension` (`facet.ts:549-552`; `design.md` §3); Q107, the no-key merge reports reason `op` (`raw/replay.jsonl`), not `type`; Q108, `prec.keys` reports reason `op`; Q109, a `{"returns": r}` compare gives Ferrum the truthiness of `r`, `static` its truthiness, `require` its truthiness after `undefined` → `true`, each citing its cm6 line, and the comment of `falsy_value` cites `facet.ts:109` (not `:118`); Q106 and Q110, nothing. Its check: `python3 tools/impl_check.py ferrum-state checks state --chunk facet`, which must still pass (no unit code exists yet, so `checks` is the right check).
2. *The U4b card* names D12 as now written (the ops of chunk `state` read `require`, filters and extenders' results with the same rule) and `design.md` §3's wrapper rule (chunk `state` builds extensions through the same `scope.rs`).
3. *The U4 implementer:* `combine_config` stays as the skeleton has it, and its doc comment gains `/// cm6 deviation: where configs conflict in more than one field, the panic names the first of them in the caller's field order, where cm6 names the first conflict in config order, see state.cm6.134.d2`.

**Confidence.** High on Q105 (a derivation and a run over every chunk agree), Q107, Q108, Q110. High on Q106's list (computed) and on keeping the per-field form; medium-high that the crates above will not want config-major order for another reason (their fixtures will show it: a conflict fixture of `ferrum-commands` that differs in the named field cites this row). Medium-high on Q109's wording for sites outside `ferrum-state`: each such site is read when its op is written, and a misread one fails a fixture.

**Reopens.** A fixture or a consumer that compares the identity of two extension wrappers around one content (Q105); a crate above whose callers catch the conflict and read the field (Q106); a site where cm6 reads a value for truthiness and in some other way on the same path (Q109).

## 2026-11-19 · Q111 to Q115: what the checks of chunk `state` infer, and the clock

*Decided by the consultant, after the U4b checks (commit b15086c). Binding text: `docs/parity/ferrum-state/plan.md` "Positions", rules 13 to 15; `design.md` §10 "Time"; `raw/decisions.jsonl` `state.cm6.105.d1` and `state.cm6.160.d1`; `raw/improvements.jsonl` `state.imp.015`. Closes Q111 to Q115.*

**The class behind three of the five.** Q111, Q112 and Q114 are one problem: the replayer must convert a number through a document that Ferrum does not hand it, so it infers the document. I read each inference against the recorded fixtures, and each is right on all of them: the 16 calls that give a `lineSeparator` together with a selection (F0118, F0137, F0424, F0425, F0428, F1318, F2008, F2010, F2033 to F2036, F2092 to F2094) each hold one literal `of`, outside any precedence; the five fixtures in which a `map` function of the program reads its mapping (F0813 to F0815 through `effect.map`, F2085 and F2086 through `update` with a first spec that has no changes) each have one pair of documents with the description's lengths. So nothing recorded fails today. What is wrong is that each inference would be wrong silently on an input nobody recorded, and conformance runs the same replayer on fresh seeds (G5). A conversion through the wrong document gives a wrong number, which usually fails, but can pass, and an error in the proof that can pass is the kind I weigh highest. The rule for all three: an inferred document is either checked against Ferrum after the call, or used only where every candidate converts alike; anything else fails the fixture as the replayer's own failure, which is a question under rule 8, never a guess.

**Q111. The separator.** cm6 splits `config.doc` at the static value of `lineSeparator` the resolved configuration holds (`mirrors/cm6/state/src/state.ts:244-246`), and the selection is checked against that document. The replayer cannot ask Ferrum first: a second `create` would call the configuration's functions again, and the program logs them. So it reads the value from the input, as it does now, with two additions: inputs that disagree, or a value of a form it does not read, together with a selection, fail as its own; and after the call it compares the lines it split with the lines of the document Ferrum created. Chunk `facet` (`create`, `scenario`, `state.fromJSON`) splits with `Form::text`, which ignores the separator; no recorded fixture of that chunk gives a separator and a selection together (F6027 gives a separator only), so the same rule is required there, not a fix of a failing case. *Lost:* modelling precedence and compartments to compute the facet's value (code that duplicates `Configuration.resolve` for a case no fixture has, and still unchecked); creating the state twice.

**Q112. The documents of a mapping.** A `ChangeDesc` carries no text. The replayer's lookup took the last pair, over the whole run, with the right byte lengths, and filler when none had them. Two pairs from one `update` can have the same lengths and different texts (doc `abc`, a first spec inserting `é` at 0, a second inserting `xy` at 0: `mergeTransaction`'s two mappings both go from 5 to 7 bytes, from `éabc` and from `xyabc`), and filler is wrong as soon as the text is not ASCII. Decision (rule 14): the pairs are those cm6's code forms at that call, the merges of `mergeTransaction` among them, scoped to the call of Ferrum in progress; ambiguity or no pair falls back to filler only when every text of the call is ASCII, where no conversion depends on the document, and fails otherwise. *Lost:* matching by the description's sections as well (exact, but it needs the replayer to rebuild each mapping in Ferrum at every site, including the filter's inverted mapping, for a gain the ASCII condition already secures on every recorded case); keeping the run-wide search (a pair from an earlier call can match).

Reading `mergeTransaction` for this I found a cm6 defect nobody had asked about: a sequential spec's own effects are mapped through `ChangeSet.empty(b.changes.length)` (`mirrors/cm6/state/src/transaction.ts:314`), the length of the document *before* that spec's changes, while the effects are positions *after* them. A valid effect at the end of the inserted text makes `update` throw. Ferrum reproduces it now (the floor, D11), and it is the candidate `state.imp.015`, evaluated in the improvement delta. Under D1 the throw cannot be reproduced exactly for non-ASCII text, because it compares a position of one document with the length of another; no recorded fixture reaches that difference, and the register row says what conformance does if a fresh seed does.

**Q113. The clock.** The question asked which reason code a time read from the clock carries. The better answer is that nothing about time needs leaving out. `now` is a harness op whose interpreter runs `Date.now()`, whole milliseconds since the Unix epoch; Rust reads the same quantity from `SystemTime`. What F0448 records from it is deterministic: the time is an integer and lies between two reads of `now` around the update. Leaving `now` out made `state.Transaction^time.B2` (the time defaults to the clock) look compared in part while nothing of it was compared. Worse, the replayer recognized a clock value by "above 1e12", so F0511, which gives `Transaction.time` 1e15 explicitly and records `1000000000000000`, was left out whole: the 1e15 case `state.Transaction^time.B1` names was compared nowhere, silently. Decision: the replayer's `now` reads the clock as `Date.now()` does, `tr.annotation` returns Ferrum's value, and the row `state.cm6.105.d1` now says the milliseconds are truncated, as `Date.now()` gives them (rounding could put the time one millisecond after the second read). `json.ann` keeps writing a time above 1e12 as the unstable `wall-clock`, because that is what the interpreter recorded. The risk is a wall clock stepping back between two reads during a test run: cm6's recording ran the same risk, and the failure would show. *Lost:* a reason `time` or rows of `raw/replay.jsonl` for `now` and `tr.annotation` (they would have answered the question as asked and kept both losses).

**Q114. A range carried to another document.** cm6 passes numbers; a range of Ferrum on one document, used on another, must stand for the same numbers in cm6's unit there. The replayer rebuilds it with `cursor` or `range` from its getters, which is right for every range those two build, but an undirectional range (`EditorSelection.undirectionalRange`, `mirrors/cm6/state/src/selection.ts:217-219`) rebuilt by `range` loses the flag and gains an `assoc` (`:209-211`). Decision (rule 15): `undirectional_range` for an undirectional range, and after building, every getter compared with the original's, a difference failing as the replayer's own. *Lost:* a constructor from raw flags (API beyond the specification for a test's sake).

**Q115. The variant for a selection whose numbers are not non-negative integers.** cm6 reads `json.selection` through `EditorSelection.fromJSON` (`mirrors/cm6/state/src/state.ts:235`), which reads each range through `SelectionRange.fromJSON` (`mirrors/cm6/state/src/selection.ts:178`); a throw inside propagates unchanged. D13's `Err` for a number outside D12's domain belongs to the same readers, so it follows the same path: the variant of the reader that reads the number, unchanged by every reader above it. A range's `anchor` or `head` gives `InvalidSelectionRangeJson`, a selection's `main` gives `InvalidEditorSelectionJson`. F1413 to F1421 all have `main: -1`, so `EditorState::from_json` returns `InvalidEditorSelectionJson`, which is what the written test asserts, what `EditorSelection::from_json` already returns (U2's code, `crates/ferrum-state/src/selection.rs`, `from_json`) and what the rustdoc of `EditorState::from_json` says. The test stands. The row `state.cm6.160.d1` now says so. *Lost:* one variant for every failure of `EditorState::from_json` (it would hide which part of the JSON was wrong, which cm6's messages tell).

**What each later step does.**
1. *A round of the U4b `test-writer`* on `crates/ferrum-state/tests/replay/` (60,000, the budget of a round; before the U4 code exists, so its check is `python3 tools/impl_check.py ferrum-state checks state`, which must pass whole; its commit is the checks commit the U4 code step takes as `--since`):
   - Q111 (rule 13): in chunk `state`, `separator_of` fails as the replayer's own on inputs that disagree or a value it cannot read when a selection is given; after `state.create` and `state.fromJSON`, the lines split are compared with `Text::to_json` of the created document wherever a position was converted through them. In chunk `facet`, `create`, `scenario` and `state.fromJSON` split the document with the separator their `ext` gives (the builtin facet under the name `defs` binds), with the same check.
   - Q112 (rule 14): the pairs of documents are kept per call of Ferrum, `update_specs` adds the pairs of each merge of `mergeTransaction`, and `mapping_value` uses filler only when the state's document and every inserted text of the call are ASCII, else fails naming the op and the two lengths.
   - Q113 (`design.md` §10 "Time"): `now` returns whole milliseconds since the Unix epoch from `SystemTime`; `tr.annotation` drops its branch above 1e12 and returns Ferrum's value; the literal tests of the harness ops cover `now` (an integer between two reads of the clock).
   - Q114 (rule 15): `RangeV::to_doc` builds an undirectional range with `undirectional_range`, compares the built value's getters with the original's, and a selection carried so also its count and main index.
   - The round's changes are listed in `implementation.md` "Changes to checks after their code", as those of b15086c are, each with the rule of `plan.md` or `design.md` it follows.
2. *The U4 implementer:* `Transaction::TIME` truncated to whole milliseconds (`state.cm6.105.d1`); `EditorState::from_json` returns `EditorSelection::from_json`'s error unchanged (`state.cm6.160.d1`); the sequential mapping of `mergeTransaction` as cm6 has it, `ChangeSet::empty` of the spec's changes' `length()` (the floor; `state.imp.015` is not adopted). Expected at its step: F0448 and F0511 `equal`.

**Confidence.** High on Q113 (the defect in F0511 is read from the code and the fixture; the clock comparison is deterministic but for a clock stepped back) and Q115 (cm6's propagation and the code already written agree). High that rules 13 to 15 cannot pass a wrong conversion silently; medium-high that they fail on no recorded fixture (each inference was checked against the recorded inputs by hand, not by a run; the round's check runs them). Medium on `state.imp.015` being better: the defect is plain in the code, the intent is inferred from `extendTransaction`.

**Reopens.** A recorded or fresh-seed fixture that rules 13 to 15 fail as the replayer's own (then the inference is extended, not dropped); a test run in which F0448 fails on the clock; a consumer that relies on a sequential spec's own effects throwing (`state.imp.015`).

## 2026-11-19 · Q116 to Q118 and the checks the code of unit `state` found wrong

*Decided by the consultant, after the U4 code (commit 9ee7049; `python3 tools/impl_check.py ferrum-state code state --since efdebd2` failing at the orchestrator's run on ebf00e1 on 87 mismatches, the `replay` test, and 23 behaviors with no check that runs). Binding text: `raw/decisions.jsonl` `state.cm6.029.d1`, `.036.d1`, `.059.d1` (changed), `state.cm6.035.d1`, `.044.d1`, `.049.d1`, `.051.d1`, `.225.d2` (new); `raw/replay.jsonl` `isInstance` of chunk `state`; `raw/improvements.jsonl` `state.imp.016`; `design.md` §3 (the row on `===` in a provider's `reconfigure`), §5 (the panic of language data, the count of implicit throws), §10 ("No counterpart": cases 1 to 5, and what a callback of a call not made writes). Closes Q116 to Q118.*

**How I sorted the 87.** I read the implementer's entry for each class and re-derived it from the fixture, cm6's code and the rule it names; for F1166, F0179 and F0477 I also ran the replayer, and the two rows I changed without touching the replayer (F0179, F0477) were run again after the change: F0179 is `equal`, F0477 `none` under its row. Three kinds came out.

*The replayer's, each restoring a rule already written* (76 of the 87, and F0232, which is `none` and not a mismatch; a round of the U4b `test-writer`):
- 19 fixtures (state F0117, F0118, F0119, F0137, F0138, F0142, F1309, F2007 to F2010, F2015, F2016, F2091 to F2094; facet F0183, F0185): `state_json` holds a `Ref` of the `RefCell` across the `borrow_mut` that writes the converted position (`tests/replay/facet/scope.rs:316-325`), so every `state.toJSON` with a range panics. The op's header.
- 4 (state F0334, F0336, F2021, F2023): `Invalid position …` of `Text::line_at` is neither named nor converted by chunk `state`'s `thrown`; chunk `text`'s does both. `plan.md` "Positions" rule 10, first row.
- 2 (state F1166, F2063): a message of `update` converted through the wrong document. Rule 10 with rule 6 settles both: in F1166 the message's 9 is the `anchor` of the third spec's `selection`, measured on the document after that spec's own changes (`acb\n\naéa`, 8 code units, 9 bytes), so Ferrum's bytes, 10 in a set of 9, convert to cm6's 9 and 8; in F2063 the sequential spec's `from: 5` is measured on `😁😀`, 4 code units. Ferrum's numbers are right.
- 47 (state F0419 to F0425, F0428 to F0436, 26 fixtures from F1057 to F1131, F1319, F2037 to F2040): `get` of `changes`, `selection` or `effects` on what `replace_selection` (a `TransactionSpec`) and `change_by_range` (a `ChangeByRange`) return. Both are public fields; the replayer has no reader for them.
- 1 (state F7259): `effect.define` with `map: 5` is excluded by type, rightly; the name it defines must then stand for no counterpart (case 5), as chunk `facet`'s names do (`scope.rs`, "The value no counterpart a name stands for"), instead of failing with `interp: no type e`.
- 1 (state F0232): `same` of two `state.facet` reads of `phrases` is given no counterpart; `design.md` §10 "Identity" compares a value read from a state by the address of the slot's value. Here that is `true`, the record.
- 3 (state F0457, F0529, F0530): a spec's `selection` that is `null`, `false` or `0`. cm6 keeps the falsy value itself (`sel && (…)`, `mirrors/cm6/state/src/transaction.ts:336`) and `tr.selection` returns it: the value is read otherwise than for truthiness, its falsy branch is not the absent value, so D12 excludes it (`type`), as the implementer read. `undefined` stays the default, `None`. The behaviors lose nothing they show in Ferrum: `state.Transaction.selection.B2` and `state.TransactionSpec.selection.B1` keep F0456 and F0525 to F0528.

*Mine, where a row or a rule was wrong or missing* (10 of the 87, and F7224, which the change of `state.cm6.029.d1` brings under its row):
- **F0179** (facet) is listed by `state.cm6.036.d1` since S5's first part (c6f4a53), and its program reads `tr.state` once: the deviation, a second `tr.state` building again, cannot show, and Ferrum's output equals the record. Removed from the row; it is compared, `equal`.
- **F0477** (state) is F0136's input in chunk `state`; it shows `state.cm6.059.d1`, whose list was computed on chunk `facet` alone. Listed; the row names `state.Transaction.state.B7`, and the written test of F0136 covers B7 and the row.
- **F0263, F7224 to F7226** and `state.cm6.029.d1`: the row said "a field holding `null` or `undefined` does not exist (D12)", and the replayer drops such a field. Against D12 as widened at Q109, that is wrong: cm6's `languageDataAt` pushes every own property, `undefined` included (`state.ts:357`, and `state.EditorState.languageDataAt.B1` says so), so it does not treat it as absent. And it is needless: a language-data value is of any type, in cm6 and in Ferrum (`Arc<dyn Any>`), and in the replayer a JavaScript `null` or `undefined` is a value of the program like any string or object it already stores. So the field is stored as given: F0263 becomes `equal`. A `wordChars` that is not a string is then what the row's condition already names, Ferrum's own code reading it as `String`; that holds for all six fixtures with such a `wordChars` (F7221 to F7226, which I found by scanning the inputs of chunks `state`, `text` and `facet`; F0643 of chunk `text` holds a lone surrogate, D12), so the row lists the six. F7221 and F7222 were filed under `type`; they are not, since the signature holds an array. `design.md` §5 counted `makeCategorizer`'s throw on `null` among the D12 throws; it is now the one Ferrum does not reach, because `char_categorizer` panics before it.
- **F0116** (facet): the `try` around `create` of an extension excluded by type gives `ok`. `design.md` §10 said that a call "was not made" in cases 1 to 4, and left out case 5, an argument that is itself no counterpart, in which Ferrum is not called either. The three consequences (arguments evaluated, what it would have changed unknown, whether cm6 threw there unknown) hold for case 5 the same way. The text now says cases 1 to 5. That can only turn compared positions into no counterpart, and only where Ferrum was not called.
- **F7109, F7110** (facet): a `computeN` getter returning a `Set` is excluded by type, and the counter its spec names is compared afterwards (0 against 2). `design.md` §10 said only that a Ferrum value the call would have changed is unknown; a value of the program that a callback of that call writes is unknown for the same reason. Added: the counter a callback spec names, and a variable a `fn` callback's body changes through a harness op.
- **F0813, F0814** (state): `isInstance(m, "ChangeSet")` of the mapping an effect type's `map` receives. cm6 declares `ChangeDesc` there (`transaction.ts:43`, `:79`) and passes a `ChangeSet`; Ferrum passes the declared type and dispatches by it (`design.md` §3, Q87, where no mirrored caller tests the class). The `isInstance` row of chunk `state` becomes `part`, naming that class asked of that mapping; the rest of both fixtures (the value, both lengths, `mapPos`) stays compared.

*Q117, F6034 (text)*, below.

**Q116. The panic of `language_data_at` for a value of another type.** `dyn Any` gives a `TypeId`, not a name, so the message cannot name the stored type without changing what `LanguageData` holds (a name beside every value, which every provider would pay for a message). The message the code writes, `Language data field "<name>" holds a value that is not a <T>`, names the field, which is what the provider and the reader agree on, and the type the reader asked for. It goes into `design.md` §5 as a panic of Ferrum's own, and the row says so. `std::any::type_name` documents that its text may change between compiler versions, so the written test compares the message with `type_name::<T>()`, not with a literal. *Lost:* the stored type in the message (a change of the public `LanguageData` type for a debugging aid); no message of Ferrum's own (cm6 hands the value over and the caller computes with it, which Rust cannot express). Confidence: high.

**Q117. A negative skip before a partial cursor's first `next`.** cm6 raises the skip to `from - pos` and `nextInner` adds `from - pos` again (`mirrors/cm6/state/src/text.ts:507-511`, `:490-493`): the cursor moves twice the distance to its range, and in F6034 `iterRange(2, 14).next(-1)` returns `two`, skipping `e `, which is inside the range. Ferrum does the same arithmetic in bytes (`state.cm6.225.d1`); doubling a distance does not commute with converting it, so on text that is not ASCII the landing differs, and inside a character Ferrum panics with its message for the value of a cursor (`design.md` §5). This is F5046's kind (`state.cm6.188.d2`): D1 changes which place cm6's arithmetic reaches, and no conversion recovers it. Decision: the row `state.cm6.225.d2` (reproduce) keeps the arithmetic and lists F6034, with a condition so that only the step that meets it is left out and the rest of the fixture (three of its four parts) stays compared: a negative skip given to a partial cursor before its first `next`, in a document with a character outside ASCII. The cursor is then no counterpart for its later steps, as any `&mut` receiver of a call not made. The double count reads as a defect of cm6 (each addition is meant to bring the cursor to the range; together they count it twice), and fixing it removes the panic too: `state.imp.016`, a candidate evaluated in the improvement delta, with its patch to run then. No mirrored package calls `next` with a negative literal. *Lost:* converting the skip so that Ferrum lands where cm6 does (Ferrum would then follow cm6's units, not its arithmetic, which is no rule a caller can state); making the improvement now (D16: a delta on a proven floor, and nobody needs it before then). Confidence: high on the row; medium on the improvement, which is unrun.

**Q118. cm6's `===` and `==` of slot values in a provider's `reconfigure`** (`mirrors/cm6/state/src/facet.ts:178-179`), which decides whether the getter runs. What cm6 compares there is identity of the values the slots hold, and what makes two outputs identical is the slot keeping its old value: `dynamicFacetSlot` keeps `oldValue` exactly when `facet.compare` says equal or the providers are unchanged (`facet.ts:244-252`). For a facet without `combine`, the output is an array cm6 builds, so `===` is "the slot kept its value", which in Ferrum is the same `Arc`: the code is right to compare only the `Arc` there. For any other output, the value is what the user's `combine` returned, and a field's value is what the user's `create` or `update` returned; D14 already says `===` on a user's value is its type's `PartialEq` (pointer equality for a type cm6 compares by identity, as the replayer's are). So the code's rule stands with one correction: the test is "the facet has no `combine`", not "the output type is `Vec<I>`". A `combine` that returns a `Vec<I>` returns the user's value, and its type's equality applies, as for every other user value; the type test would treat it as Ferrum's array. Written into `design.md` §3. A written test pins the case the fixtures cannot reach (a list output recombined equal in content under a `compare` that says unequal: the getter runs, as in cm6). *Lost:* `Arc` identity for every output (a user type with value equality would then see a getter run where its own `==` says nothing changed, against D14); content equality for every output (a getter skipped where cm6 calls it, for the default lists Ferrum builds). Confidence: high; reopens on a consumer above that compares a combined output by identity.

**The 23 behaviors with no check that runs.** Twelve follow from the classes above: `state.StateField^define config StateFieldSpec.toJSON.B1`, `.fromJSON.B1`, `.fromJSON.B3` (the `toJSON` defect), `state.EditorState.replaceSelection.B6`, `.changeByRange.B3` to `.B6` (`get`), `state.EditorState^phrases.B2` (F0232), `state.Facet.computeN.B8` (its four fixtures then excluded by type, which the tool accepts), `state.Transaction.state.B7` (the written test's `// covers:`). For the others:
- `state.StateCommand.B1`: a written test, D5's contract as cm6's tests use it: a function and a closure, each given a `StateCommandTarget` literal, one dispatching a transaction built from the target's state and returning `true`, one returning `false` without dispatching when `readOnly` is set.
- `state.StateCommand.B2`: behavior of `@codemirror/view`'s keymap, not of this package; row `state.cm6.035.d1` (`form`) says it is specified and checked with `ferrum-view`.
- `state.FacetReader.B3`, `state.StateField.extension.B6`, `.B7`, `state.Prec.B6`: JavaScript's dynamic objects (a reader shaped by hand, a redefined property, a second copy of the package, a wrapped non-extension) that Rust's types cannot express; rows `state.cm6.044.d1`, `.049.d1`, `.051.d1` (`form`). Their ops already have their rows in `raw/replay.jsonl`; the rows say what has no behavior. `state.Prec.B6`'s one fixture will be `part` after F0116 is fixed, on a label the program wrote, so the tool would count it compared; the row records that nothing of Ferrum is.
- `state.EditorState.selection.B1`, `state.Transaction.newSelection.B1`, `state.StateEffect^mapEffects.B1`, `state.StateEffect^reconfigure.B4`, `state.StateEffect^appendConfig.B3`: each states object identity of a value Ferrum makes a value with none (`design.md` §10, "Identity"), and its fixtures record only `same`. The counterpart in Ferrum is equality of the value: written tests assert it (the state's selection after an empty update and after a change that does not move it equals the start selection; `new_selection` equals the selection the spec gave; `map_effects` of an empty list is empty; a `reconfigure` or `append_config` effect mapped through changes is returned with its value, the same extension by `same_extension`).
- `state.Compartment.reconfigure.B1`: the effect's type and value are private in Ferrum (`implementation.md` "Native mechanisms"); a written test shows the public part: the effect, mapped through changes, still reconfigures the compartment, and the content is the extension given, not a copy.
- `state.EditorState.charCategorizer.B5` will join the list once its six fixtures are under `state.cm6.029.d1`: the written test of the panic (Q116) covers it and the row.

**What else I checked, unasked.** The code step listed two cases of cm6 with no behavior row (`implementation.md` "Cases of cm6 with no behavior row", unit state). I ran each on a patched copy of the harness's cm6 over chunks `facet` and `state` (one patch each, on scratch copies, nothing kept). *`facet.ts:164`*, `ensureAll` skipped when the document or selection condition holds: with `ensureAll` called first, 11 fixtures of chunk `facet` change (F5071, F5074, F5083, F5084, F5099, F5102, F5107, F5108, F5113, F5114, F5122). Pinned; no row states it otherwise; nothing to do. *`facet.ts:177-179`*, `every` stopping at the first dependency that differs: with every dependency read, no fixture changes. It is observable: with facets defined in the order A, P, B, a provider of P computed from `[A, B]` and B computed from `[A]`, reconfiguring A's compartment calls P's getter before B's on cm6 6.7.6 and B's before P's on the patched copy (a program run on both). Ferrum ports `every`, but nothing checks it, and the order of callbacks is behavior (D14). So step 3 of U4 runs on this one case: `state.Facet.compute.B8` states the condition, and is corrected to state the order, with that program as its fixture.

**What it requires.**
1. *A round of the U4b `test-writer`* (`crates/ferrum-state/tests/`; budget 140,000: a round of a wrong check is 60,000, and this one also carries ten written tests and one row condition; the stop-loss of `docs/workflow/README.md` §5 counts it as one round). It is given this entry, the traces named in `implementation.md` "Checks believed wrong", unit state, and no code. Each change is listed in `implementation.md` "Changes to checks after their code" with the rule it restores:
   - `state_json` writes the converted positions without holding a borrow across the write (19 fixtures);
   - chunk `state`'s `thrown` names and converts `Invalid position P in document of length L` as chunk `text`'s does (rule 10);
   - `update`'s messages converted through the argument rule 10 picks with rule 6 (F1166: the `selection` of the third spec, on the document after its own changes; F2063: the sequential spec's `from`, on `😁😀`), and if a recorded message fits no argument, the fixture fails as the replayer's own (rule 8), never a guess;
   - `get` of `changes`, `selection` and `effects` on a `TransactionSpec` and a `ChangeByRange` Ferrum returned, through their public fields;
   - a falsy `selection` of a spec other than `undefined` makes the call no counterpart, `type`, with the value (D12);
   - the name an `effect.define` excluded by type defines stands for no counterpart (case 5);
   - `same` of values read from a state by `state.facet` and `state.field` follows the row "a value read from a state" of `design.md` §10 "Identity" (F0232);
   - the language-data provider stores a field holding `null` or `undefined` as the program's value, and reads every `wordChars` that is not a string under the condition of `state.cm6.029.d1` (no `type` branch left for it there);
   - `isInstance` of chunk `state` gives no counterpart for the class `ChangeSet` asked of the mapping Ferrum gives an effect type's `map` (`raw/replay.jsonl`);
   - `design.md` §10 as now written: case 5 is a call not made (F0116's `try`); the counter a callback spec names and the variables a `fn` body writes are no counterpart after a call not made, or a call during which a callback returned what the signature cannot hold (F7109, F7110);
   - chunk `text`'s cursor op applies the condition of `state.cm6.225.d2` (F6034): a negative skip before a partial cursor's first `next` in a document with a character outside ASCII leaves that step out and the cursor no counterpart after it;
   - written tests in `crates/ferrum-state/tests/written_state.rs`, each with its `// covers:`: `state.StateCommand.B1`; `state.Compartment.reconfigure.B1`; the value counterparts of `state.EditorState.selection.B1`, `state.Transaction.newSelection.B1`, `state.StateEffect^mapEffects.B1`, `state.StateEffect^reconfigure.B4`, `state.StateEffect^appendConfig.B3`; the panic of `language_data_at` and of `char_categorizer` on a `wordChars` that is not a string, the message compared with `type_name::<T>()` (`state.EditorState.charCategorizer.B5`, `state.cm6.029.d1`); the getter of a provider that depends on a facet without `combine` whose `compare` says unequal, run after a reconfiguration that recombines an equal list (`state.Facet.compute.B8`, Q118); and the test of F0136 also covers `state.Transaction.state.B7` and `state.cm6.059.d1`.
   - Its check: `python3 tools/impl_check.py ferrum-state code state --since efdebd2`, whose replay must report no mismatch; the behaviors it still lists as having no check are reported, not worked around. The U4 implementer's change below may be made first; if it is not, the Q118 test fails until it is, which the report says.
2. *The U4 implementer* (budget 40,000): `FacetOps::same_output` compares an output only by `Arc` when the facet has no `combine`, and otherwise by `Arc` or `O`'s `==`, whatever `O` is (Q118, `design.md` §3); the `# Panics` of `language_data_at` says the message names the field and the type asked for (Q116, `design.md` §5); `implementation.md`: "Native mechanisms", unit state, the line on `===` and `==` as now decided, and the implicit throw of `charcategory.ts:32` as the one not reached (`state.cm6.029.d1`).
3. *A `spec-auditor` at step 3 of U4* (budget 80,000, one case): corrects `state.Facet.compute.B8` from `mirrors/cm6/state/src/facet.ts:177-179` to state that the check stops at the first dependency that is not the same, so that a later dependency's slot is computed later, when the getter or its own place in the order reaches it (with `added: implementation state`), and records its fixture through chunk `facet`'s interpreter from a generator `oracle/cm6/facet/impl-state.ts`: facets defined in the order A, P, B, P computed from `[A, B]` with a getter that does not read B, B computed from `[A]`, A in a compartment, the compartment reconfigured, the order of the two getters logged (`["P getter", "B getter"]` on cm6). An op it adds gets its header line and rows of `raw/positions.jsonl`; the U4b `test-writer` registers it. Check: `python3 tools/spec_check.py ferrum-state` passes at the worker's run and the orchestrator's. Then I read the corrected row.
4. *The orchestrator:* the order above, one worker at a time (implementer, `test-writer`, `spec-auditor`); its cards name this entry; after my reading of step 3, `python3 tools/impl_check.py ferrum-state code state --since efdebd2` at its own run; the progress file; the ledger rows of each step. If the new fixture of step 3 fails on Ferrum, the implementer is resumed with its trace.

**Confidence.** High on the sorting: each replayer defect restores a rule already written, and each row change was either run (F0179, F0477) or read from cm6's code and the fixture's input (029.d1). Medium-high on the added rule for what a callback of a call not made writes: it is the right direction (it can only report less), and the round will show whether the variables a `fn` body writes can be found from its program as simply as I expect; if not, that part is a question.

## 2026-11-19 · Q119 and what remains after U4

*Decided by the consultant, after the rounds of "Q116 to Q118 and the checks the code of unit `state` found wrong" (implementer b5b36df, `test-writer` 4386fc7, `spec-auditor` e04abe2; `python3 tools/impl_check.py ferrum-state code state --since efdebd2` passing whole at the orchestrator's run on 427a43e, `spec_check` passing on f263faa). Binding text: `design.md` §3 (the row on whether a facet has a `combine` of its own), §10 ("No counterpart", what a callback of a call not made writes); `.claude/rules/rust-naming-decisions.md` N17; `plan.md` "After U4", which points here. Closes Q119.*

**Step 3 read.** `state.Facet.compute.B8` now states what `mirrors/cm6/state/src/facet.ts:177-179` does: the dependencies are checked in list order, `every` stops at the first one that is not the same, and reading a dependency for the check computes its slot, so a later dependency is computed when the getter or the slot order reaches it. That is the code I read again; the row carries `added: implementation state` and names F7400 both ways. F7400 is the program my entry asked for (A, P, B in that order, P from `[A, B]` with a getter reading A only, B from `[A]`, A in a compartment reconfigured from 1 to 2), and its log `["get P","get B","get P","get B"]` discriminates: the first two calls are `EditorState.create`'s slot order, the same under either reading; the last two are the reconfiguration's, and the auditor's probe on a cm6 whose `every` does not stop gives `["get P","get B","get B","get P"]`. The generator `oracle/cm6/facet/impl-state.ts` runs after `s4-fix6.ts` in `order.json` and keeps its range. Nothing to change. Ferrum gives `equal`.

**Q119. Two thread-local cells in `crates/ferrum-state/src/facet.rs`.** D6 allows no global mutable state but id counters. Its reason is that a value of the foundation behaves the same wherever and whenever it is used; a flag set and restored inside one call breaks that whenever a callback runs inside the window. I looked for such a path and found one for `DEFAULT_COMBINE_RAN`: it is set by *any* run of the default `combine`, and `Facet::define` calls the caller's `combine([])` inside its window, so a `combine` that builds an `EditorState` there with any facet defined by `FacetConfig::default()` provided (a caller's, or `EditorState::LANGUAGE_DATA`, `CHANGE_FILTER`) sets it, and the facet is classed as having no `combine` of its own; so does a nested `define` whose `combine` panics after a default one ran, caught by `catch_unwind`, which skips the restore. The consequence is Q118's rule applied to the wrong facet, a getter call cm6 does not make or the reverse: an order of callbacks, observable and silent. The cases are contrived; the rule is not, and the next crates will meet the same temptation. `IN_BUILTINS` has no such path (only the crate's own code runs while it is set), but it is a flag standing in for a parameter.

*Decision.* Both cells go. `IN_BUILTINS`: the built-in facets take their ids from the counter through a private function that does not force `BUILTINS`. `DEFAULT_COMBINE_RAN`: the default `combine` is told by its type. The public field `FacetConfig.combine` becomes `Box<dyn CombineFn<I, O>>`, `CombineFn<I, O>` a public trait with the supertraits `Fn(&[I]) -> O + Any + Send + Sync`, implemented for every such closure; `FacetConfig::default()` holds the closure of one private generic function, and `Facet::define` compares the `TypeId` of the boxed closure, upcast to `&dyn Any`, with that function's closure for the same `I`. I compiled this on rustc 1.97 in a scratch file with `#![deny(warnings)]` and ran clippy on it, both clean: struct update syntax from `FacetConfig::default()` keeps the default (`true`); `Box::new(|v| v.to_vec())` with no type written is inferred and is a `combine` of its own (`false`); `FacetConfig::new` and a non-list output are `false`. The trait is named by N17's new pattern, `<Member>Fn`, and annotated `/// ferrum-only: the type of FacetConfig.combine, see N17`. D6 stays as written.

*Why.* The type of the closure is a fact of the value, fixed when the configuration is written; no callback, panic or thread can change it, which is what D6 asks. It also answers exactly cm6's own question, `!config.combine` (`facet.ts:73`): a caller who writes a `combine`, even one doing what the default does, has one. Callers write the same code: the replayer, the ported tests and the written tests use `FacetConfig::new` or struct update syntax and name neither the field's type nor the alias, so no check changes.

*Lost.* Keeping the cells and naming "a flag set and restored within one call" in D6: an exception with a failure mode found on the first look, in the rule every later crate reads. Making `combine` an `Option` (cm6 does tell its absence apart, so D14 would allow it): `None` with an output that is not the list of inputs is then writable and must panic at run time, which `design.md` §3 excluded by type (D12). A private field in `FacetConfig` marking the default: struct update syntax from outside the crate does not compile on a struct with a private field (E0451), and it is the form D14 gives callers. Comparing the vtable or the data pointer of the box: not guaranteed unique, and a zero-sized closure's data pointer is shared by all of them.

*Confidence.* High: compiled, and the behavior it serves (Q118) is unchanged. *Reopens.* A public signature the trait makes harder to write than the alias did, shown by a caller.

**The core change, `Program::not_made`, stands.** It counts a call not made for case 5 of `design.md` §10 and keeps the excluded argument's entry as the last call not made, so that a `try` or `capture` around it is no counterpart (F0116), which is the rule my entry on Q116 to Q118 wrote. It is in the language module and imports nothing of `ferrum_state`; `python3 tools/impl_check.py ferrum-state core` passes at my run on f263faa (48 language cases, control run at 0, 48 traces equal to cm6's, no `ferrum_state` in `tests/replay/lang`). Two things follow. The plan asks a literal test of each rule of §10 ("The replayer", "Its own tests"), and case 5 through `not_made` has none in `tests/replay/rules.rs`. And an uncaught error after a case-5 call gets an entry `error` that names the op of the excluded argument, not the call not made; `handoff`'s check of error entries (`tools/impl_check.py` `error_entries`) accepts the named op only at cm6's throw or inside it, so where the excluded value came through a variable that check fails. That failure is visible and may not occur in the 4,965 fixtures; if it does, the fix is in the core, below.

**The reading of the variables a `fn` writes stands, with two precisions.** The `test-writer` took every name an `as` binds and every variable an `array.push` grows, in the body of every `fn` evaluated so far in the run, and makes them no counterpart after any call not made of chunk `state`. Which `fn` a call would reach through the configuration it is given (a field's `update` added two extensions earlier) is not something the program shows simply, and the broad set can only compare less, never pass a wrong value. I measured what it costs: of the 1,128 fixtures of chunk `state`, the only one with a call not made and a `fn` that writes is F7250, where `seen` is written by the `transactionFilter` the excluded `state.update` would have called: the case the rule exists for. Every `array.push` inside a `fn` in the five chunks (50, all in chunk `state`) pushes onto a variable named by `$`, so the reading misses nothing recorded. The two precisions, for programs not recorded yet (conformance G5 runs the same replayer on fresh seeds): the `params` of the `fn` are written by each call too, so they belong to the set; and a name is made no counterpart whether or not it held a value (the code skips one that is unset, so a variable first written by the callback would be read later as "unset variable", a failure of the replayer where cm6 has a value). `design.md` §10 now says this, and drops the `set` it named, which is an op of chunk `facet`, not of the language.

**What it requires.** One worker at a time, in this order. The `--since` of `handoff` is `text=c0ba180,change=ea896a1,rangeset=126dad7,state=efdebd2`.
1. *The U4 implementer* (budget 30,000): `crates/ferrum-state/src/facet.rs` as decided for Q119 (`design.md` §3): no `thread_local!` and no `Cell` flag; `CombineFn` with its doc comment and annotation; `implementation.md` "Native mechanisms", unit state: the line on the built-in facets and the line on `===` say how each is now done. Check: `python3 tools/impl_check.py ferrum-state code state --since efdebd2` passes whole, and `grep -rn thread_local crates/` finds nothing.
2. *The U4b `test-writer`* (budget 40,000; a change I decided, not a check found wrong, listed in `implementation.md` "Changes to checks after their code" with this entry as the rule restored): in `tests/replay/rules.rs`, literal tests of case 5 through `Program::not_made` (a `try` and a `capture` around a call whose argument is no counterpart give the result and entries the rule gives; a value the program wrote before such a call is compared, and one a `fn` writes is not); in chunk `state`, the variables of a `fn` as `design.md` §10 now says (`params` included, a name made no counterpart whether set or not). Check: `python3 tools/impl_check.py ferrum-state core` and `code state --since efdebd2` pass whole.
3. *The orchestrator*, all of it a tool's: `code state --since efdebd2` at its own run; the replayer on the five chunks with `FERRUM_REPLAY_WRITE_NO_COUNTERPART=1`, and `fixtures/no-counterpart.jsonl` committed alone; the lines and branches no check reaches (`plan.md` "Hand-off": `cargo llvm-cov -p ferrum-state --features __test --ignore-run-fail --no-fail-fast`, then `cargo llvm-cov report --show-missing-lines`), as a list by file with each line's code, U1's included; `python3 tools/impl_check.py ferrum-state handoff --since …`, its output kept whole. Nothing in this step is fixed by hand.

If `handoff`'s check of error entries fails on an entry that names the excluded argument of a case-5 call, the decision is already this: `Program::not_made` takes the name of the op whose call was not made and the entry names that op, with a literal test, by the U4b `test-writer`, who wrote it.
5. *The rounds that call names*, each with its budget there.

**D12 widened: a primitive cm6 reads only through conversion to a string.** `phrase` returns `insert[n - 1]` from a `replace` callback (`mirrors/cm6/state/src/state.ts:327-331`), which JavaScript converts to a string; every mirrored caller of `phrase` with an argument passes a number (`mirrors/cm6/search/src/search.ts:519`, `:546`; `mirrors/cm6/merge/src/deco.ts:252`; `mirrors/cm6/merge/src/unified.ts:219`), and those are the inputs the fixtures recorded. Ferrum's `phrase` takes `&[&str]`, so the Rust caller writes `n.to_string()`, which for an integer is the string JavaScript makes. That is Q109's reason exactly: a rule of the language callers rely on, whose result a Rust caller writes. So a primitive (number, boolean, `null`, `undefined`) cm6 reads only through that conversion is the string it gives; an object stays excluded, since its conversion calls its own methods. *Lost:* keeping it excluded (the realistic call compared nowhere, and the same question at every `phrase` of `@codemirror/view`); `insert: &[&dyn Display]` so that a caller passes the number itself (a `&str` element then needs `&&"x"`, so the common call gets no simpler; not registered as an improvement). *Confidence:* high. *Reopens:* a site where cm6 converts a primitive and also reads it otherwise on the same path.
