## 2026-11-19 · Q120, the line `Line::is_empty`, and branch coverage at conformance

*Decided by the consultant on the orchestrator's runs after the last round (06065fa) and the rewritten `fixtures/no-counterpart.jsonl` (03880da), and on the maintainer's instruction of 2026-11-19 (`dev/maintainer/instructions/2026-11-19-ambiente-nao-e-criterio.md`). Binding text: `docs/parity/ferrum-state/design.md` §10 (the `error` entry); `tools/impl_check.py` `error_entries`; `docs/parity/ferrum-state/plan.md` "Hand-off" and "Conformance"; `docs/parity/ferrum-state/implementation.md` "Lines no check reaches".*

**Q120: an `error` entry names no op.**

*The question.* After a call Ferrum did not make, cm6's recorded run ends in an uncaught error and Ferrum's does not. The replayer leaves the error out with an entry of reason `error`, and that entry named an op. `handoff` checked two things against cm6's trace: that cm6 throws at an evaluation Ferrum did not make (the condition `design.md` §10 states), and that the op the entry names is that evaluation or a call inside it. My entry "The end of the implementation stage" decided the entry names the call not made; the round tried four rules for which one, and the best held for 117 of 143.

*What I found.* I ran cm6's trace of all 143 fixtures and read, for each, the evaluation cm6 throws in and Ferrum's trace at the same index (30 seconds of tool; the grouping is now printed by `handoff`). The first condition holds for all 143: in each, Ferrum's trace holds that evaluation, the same op, as *no counterpart*, with the reason `type` (118), `char` (12), or the row `state.cm6.160.d1` (11) or `state.cm6.029.d1` (2), all of them input Ferrum was not given or a value it does not produce, decided in their rows. Where cm6 throws in the chain is not fixed by the chain's shape: in facet F7114 at the first call that takes the excluded value (`create`), in F7138 at a later one (`tr.state`, after `create` and `state.facet` read the value without throwing), and in F7131 at the excluded call itself. The fixture records only `{error: {name, message}}`, not where the run ended. So the replayer cannot know which call not made cm6 threw in, and any op it writes is a guess: the 26 the best rule missed are not a defect of that rule but of asking the question there. My earlier entry read 44 failures and generalized; I had not run the rule against the 143 traces before deciding it.

*Decision.* The `error` entry names no op: it is written `{"reason": "error", "op": null, "part": "the uncaught error", "value": null}`. The replayer adds it, as before, when the recorded output is an uncaught error, a call was not made, and Ferrum's run does not end in that error. The code that exists only to choose the entry's op is removed (the `carried` field, `Program::carry`, the renaming in `Program::not_made`, and what calls them for that purpose); what counts calls not made, which decides whether a `try` or a `capture` is compared, stays as it is. `handoff` (changed in this commit) keeps the first condition, fails on an entry that names an op, and prints the origins grouped by chunk, the op cm6 throws in and the reason Ferrum's trace gives there (38 groups), which is what a reader of the left-out file needs and what the op was meant to tell them. Seen on the traces at 4d866bd: 143 of 143 hold with no op; with the ops of 03880da, 0 of 143; with Ferrum's trace of facet F7138 edited so that `tr.state` holds a value, 142 of 143.

*Why.* What the reason `error` must not hide is a call Ferrum made where cm6 threw; the first condition is exactly that test, run on cm6's own trace. The op added no evidence to it: it was a label, and a label the replayer cannot compute is a known-wrong value in an approved file. The grouping printed from the trace gives the reader the true origin of every entry, which no label could.

*Lost.* (a) Keep the 117-rule and list the 26 as exceptions: a frozen file with 26 wrong labels, and the rule's code (`carried`, `carry`) carried for nothing. (b) An entry listing every call not made: it always contains the origin when the first condition holds, so it proves nothing more, and it changes with every evaluation after the point where cm6 stopped. (c) Record the index cm6's run stopped at in each fixture: it re-records fixtures (E8) to fix a label. (d) Have the tool write the origin into the file: two writers of one file the replayer must equal.

*Confidence.* High: the first condition was checked on all 143, and both seeded defects fail it. *Reopens:* a fixture of G5's fresh seeds whose `error` entry's origin is an evaluation Ferrum made.

**The line `text.rs` 855-857 is `Line::is_empty`.** My sort named it `Text::is_empty`, and the round tested that function, which other checks already reached. It needs a written test, as every public form nothing calls: an empty line and a line of one character. The sort is corrected in `implementation.md`.

**Branch coverage at conformance, decided on its merits.** The maintainer's instruction: a criterion chosen because the environment lacks something is never adopted; what the work needs is asked of him; no check becomes mandatory by that. My "Conformance" plan took lines and mutation in place of branches because no nightly toolchain was installed, and called mutation a stand-in for branches. Re-derived:
- *What each shows.* Line coverage shows code no check runs. A branch outcome shows a condition no check ever made true, or never false: a line can run every time with its `if` never skipping, which is a case nobody ran, the kind of error G2's coverage exists for. Mutation asks a different question, whether the checks *forbid* a change to code they run, and on 19 named functions only; it does not stand for branches, and branches do not stand for it.
- *What it costs.* I ran it at 4d866bd (`cargo +nightly llvm-cov -p ferrum-state --features __test --no-fail-fast --branch`, rustc 1.102.0-nightly of 2026-11-18): every test passed; the report counts 1,336 branch outcomes, 56 missed; summed over the instantiations of generic functions, which its JSON lists apart, that is 670 conditions, 55 of them with an outcome never taken. A few minutes of a tool, and 55 items to read.
- *What it found that lines did not.* Among the 55: `Text`'s equality never compared two texts whose iterators differ in a line break or in `done` (`text.rs:744`); `eq_including_assoc` of a range never false (`selection.rs:146`); `Transaction::annotation`'s type test never false (`transaction.rs:52`); the re-entrant read of a transaction's lazy state never taken (`transaction.rs:577`); `RangeSet::compare`'s value comparison of active ranges never different (`rangeset.rs:825`). Some will turn out to be outcomes no input reaches; each is a question lines cannot ask.

So G2's coverage is lines and branches, in one run on the nightly toolchain; mutation stays as written. Who reads the 55: the unit's verifier at conformance, who reads the unit's code and checks and its surviving mutants anyway, each outcome either an input no check runs (a finding, with the input) or an outcome no input reaches (with what excludes it). Lines the nightly run lists that the sort does not (it lists 46: `rangeset.rs` 1155 and 1611, `state.rs` 329 besides the stable 43, brace and region attribution of another compiler) go the same way. Not sorted by me now: I would read 55 outcomes at speed beside the code, where the verifiers read them with the unit; and nothing in the hand-off depends on them. *Lost:* lines only (a cheaper report that misses exactly the conditions above); condition-level coverage (MC/DC: whether each operand of a condition changes its outcome), not weighed further, since mutation of the named functions asks that of the code where an operand matters most and costs nothing more to set up. *Confidence:* high on adopting branches; medium on the verifiers' extra 10,000 each. *Reopens:* the verifiers' reading finding every missed outcome unreachable, which would leave branch coverage for `ferrum-view` to be weighed again by its cost. The toolchain's line is in `dev/maintainer/ambiente.md` for the maintainer; until it is in the setup script, the orchestrator installs it with that line.

**What it requires.**
1. *The U4b `test-writer`, resumed* (budget 30,000): the `error` entry with `"op": null` and the code that chose its op removed, as decided above, the literal test of `rules.rs` asserting the entry with no op; a written test of `Line::is_empty` (`text.rs` 855-857); both listed in `implementation.md` "Changes to checks after their code" with the rule restored (`design.md` §10 as now written; the sort). Its check: `python3 tools/impl_check.py ferrum-state handoff --since text=c0ba180,change=ea896a1,rangeset=126dad7,state=efdebd2`, every check passing but "what the replayer leaves out equals no-counterpart.jsonl", whose differences are exactly the `op` of the 143 `error` entries; the results per chunk unchanged.
2. *The orchestrator*: `fixtures/no-counterpart.jsonl` written again and committed alone once its diff against 03880da is exactly those 143 `op` values, now `null` (anything else comes back to me); `cargo llvm-cov` as in "Hand-off", every line listed being one the sort names as no input reaches; `handoff` passing whole; then the rest of item 2 of "The end of the implementation stage" (the grouped list of `type` and `char` entries, beside the `error` origins `handoff` now prints; the ledger's `tokens` confirmed; the ledger rows; the progress file; the stage report). Conformance then starts as `plan.md` "Conformance" now says.

## 2026-11-24 · Q121 to Q129: the maintainer's review before conformance

*Decided by the consultant on the maintainer's review of 2026-11-20 (recorded in English by the orchestrator as Q121 to Q129). Binding text: `docs/parity/ferrum-state/plan.md` "Conformance" (step 0, steps 1, 2, 3 and 5); `docs/parity/ferrum-view/questions.md` Q3. Q128 changed D13 (`.claude/rules/decisions.md`; record: `docs/decisions/foundation.md`, "Q128"); Q129 is a rule of the method (`docs/workflow/README.md` §4; record: `docs/decisions/foundation.md`, "Q129"). Ledger: E43.*

**How I read the seven questions on conformance.** Q121 to Q127 ask, each from one side, whether a conforming verdict could hide a divergence: at a boundary between crates, in a conversion of values, in identity or concurrency, in errors, at the limits of input, in the API's surface, or in the checks themselves. I did not answer them by pointing at the gates. For each I asked where a divergence of that kind would go unseen with the plan as written, and changed the plan only where the answer was "somewhere". None of them blocks the start of conformance once the plan is changed.

**Q121, coherence across crates.** *Found.* Every capability `ferrum-state` offers to the crates above is a row of `raw/xc.jsonl`: 1,017, each tied to an item (184 for `ferrum-view`, 116 for `ferrum-commands`, 98 for `ferrum-language`, the rest for packages beyond the foundation). The plan of conformance never ran G10. And the crates above will run their own fixtures through the real `ferrum-state`, never through a stand-in (fakes exist only for the layout and syntax contracts, `ferrum-foundation.md` §2), so a value they read differently from cm6 fails their replay: such an error is late, not silent. *Decision.* G10 runs as a join (every `xc` row's item has its counterpart and every behavior of it a passing check), and the contracts `ferrum-state`'s design left to the crates above are gathered where `ferrum-view`'s S0 reads them (`docs/parity/ferrum-view/questions.md` Q3, eight points, among them `RangeSet` and `PartialEq` and the catching of panics of Q128). The verdict of `ferrum-state` claims parity of this crate as cm6's packages call it; it does not claim the parity of the system, which is shown crate by crate, each on the real crates below. I write that sentence into the acceptance. *Lost.* Holding the verdict until the crates above exist: it would test nothing more now, and stop the work for months. A reading of every call form of the 1,017 rows now: the skeleton's approval already read the forms of the frequent calls (`mapPos`, `findColumn`, transaction specs), and the rest is read at `ferrum-view`'s skeleton, where a mismatch costs a change under Q129's rule.

**Q122, values that lose a distinction in Rust.** *Found.* Every way a JavaScript value can lose a distinction in Ferrum already has one rule and one check that fails: what is excluded by type (D12) is an entry of the approved left-out file, and a new kind in G5's fresh seeds is a finding by the plan; absent versus a default (D14) is `Option` exactly where cm6 tells them apart; values JSON cannot hold have one encoding (Q40); values in a list of mixed types are read through their type (Q64); key order is no promise (Q61). What no check showed is that a field the replayer reports as compared is compared: the control run alters one path per fixture, chosen by a seed, so a comparator that skipped a field appearing in few fixtures could pass it. *Decision.* An exhaustive control run: every compared path of every fixture altered in turn against the same Ferrum output (step 0, a small change to the driver, by the test-writer who owns it; step 1 runs it; required: no altered path passes). It costs no new execution of Ferrum, only comparisons. *Lost.* More seeds of the sampled control (still a sample); leaving it to the reading of the replayer's core (a reading, where a run can count every path).

**Q123, identity, caches and concurrency.** *Found.* D6 is asserted at compile time for some types in the written tests, not for every public type. Outside `__test`, the crate's shared state is: the id counter (`facet.rs:24`), the built-in facets behind `LazyLock` (`extension.rs:119-130`, `facet.rs:109`, `:153`), and the `OnceLock`s a definition fills once (`facet.rs:290`, `:618`). G7 reads identity and caches, and nothing reads what two threads reaching one of these first at once could change. *Decision.* G1 checks from rustdoc JSON that every public type is `Send` and `Sync` (seen to fail on a seeded type first); step 1 lists the shared state by unit and each verifier says whether a race on first access could change an id's order, how many times a user callback runs, or an identity, a finding with the test that would show it where it could. I expect none (ids are only compared, the `OnceLock`s are filled before the value escapes), but that is a deduction, not a check. *Lost.* A stress test with threads on every type: most hold no shared state, and the list says which do.

**Q124, errors across crate boundaries.** *Found.* Inside `ferrum-state`, every recorded throw is compared (143 `error` entries checked against cm6's trace; a panic other than the three foreseen engine messages is compared as an error, `design.md` §10 "Engine errors"); G9's list of `unwrap`, `expect` and `panic!` is read by the verifiers. Across crates, D13 binds every crate, and the crates above replay through the real `ferrum-state`, so an error converted or swallowed in a layer fails that layer's fixtures. *Decision.* No change to conformance. What was missing was the rule for the layers above, which is Q128. *Lost.* A gate now for crates that do not exist.

**Q125, limits, large documents, edge cases.** *Found.* The shape checklist of every generator (`specification.md` §5: empty, boundaries, consumer and extreme values, ASCII to astral, CRLF, a case 100 times the typical size) is counted on G5's fresh seeds, an entry with no case failing; Q95 (`ChangeSet.filter` above 1e9) and `EditorSelection.asSingle.B2` have recorded outcomes. Three gaps: the typical size of a generator is small (six code units in the categorizer's), so "100 times" is no large document; `state.imp.010` (long lines) is to be evaluated at G8 "on a benchmark with long lines", which the plan's G8 did not ask for, so G6's check of candidate points would have found it past and unevaluated; and nothing read the arithmetic on sentinels (`1e9`, `usize::MAX`), where a debug build panics and a release build wraps. *Decision.* G8's documents reach 1,000,000 lines at 100n, and it benchmarks lines of 10^5 to 10^7 bytes on both sides; I settle `state.imp.010` from those times at the acceptance. Step 1 lists every sentinel and the arithmetic on it, and each verifier says whether a value the types allow can overflow or wrap there. *Lost.* Fixtures of large documents for G5: cm6's results on a large document are the same code paths as on a deep enough one, and branch coverage shows a path no case reaches; size is a question of cost, which G8 measures.

**Q126, differences of API surface.** *Found.* G1 checks the annotations and the names; the skeleton was approved by reading every signature against cm6's callers; and after it, signatures changed only through questions (Q87, Q97, Q100, Q110, Q115). Nothing checks that no signature changed otherwise. *Decision.* G1 lists every public signature that differs between the approved skeleton (2881c16) and the recorded commit; each must name the question that changed it, and I read the list at the acceptance. The consumer side is Q121's G10 and `ferrum-view` Q3. *Lost.* Reading the whole API again: the reading was done once against every caller form, and only the differences since need it.

**Q127, the sensitivity of the checks.** *Found.* The plan already measures sensitivity four ways: the control run, G5's five deliberate deviations, mutation, line and branch coverage; a sixth verifier re-checks 10 percent of the verdicts. Two weaknesses: the control run was a sample (Q122), and the mutation run could be cut to a shard `1/n` to fit six hours, which is a sample chosen by wall time. cargo-mutants' operators (a body replaced by a default, an operator swapped, a condition negated, an arm deleted) stand for local faults; faults of an algorithm are what G5's deliberate deviations and the oracle stand for. The 29 mismatches of the specification's sample were fixed in S4 and verified (`verification.md`); whatever of them could remain would fail a gate now, whatever its history. *Decision.* The exhaustive control (Q122), and every mutant of the named functions runs, in the background, however long it takes. Mutation stays on those 19 functions: the crate has 2,606 mutants (counted with `cargo mutants --list`), and the cost of a wider run is the reading of its survivors, which I decide after seeing the survival rate of this one. *Lost.* Mutation of the whole crate now (a reading of perhaps hundreds of survivors before knowing whether they find anything); keeping the shard.

**Confidence.** High that the changes close the places where a divergence could go unseen that I found; medium that I found all of them, which is what the acceptance's "what the verdict does not claim" is for. **Reopens.** A divergence found after the verdict, in `ferrum-view`'s replay or by a consumer, that one of these gates should have shown.

**Raised by me, not asked.** The plan's conformance never ran G10, and its G8 did not evaluate `state.imp.010` at the point the register names; both are fixed above.

## 2026-11-24 · Q130 to Q134: the gates of step 1 of conformance

*Decided by the consultant (Q130 to Q134, raised by the orchestrator on 2026-11-24 at the recorded commit c08e6da). Binding text: `.claude/rules/decisions.md` D6 (amended, record in `docs/decisions/foundation.md`); `docs/parity/ferrum-state/design.md` §6; `.claude/rules/rust-crates.md` "Annotations"; `docs/parity/ferrum-state/plan.md` "Conformance" step 1 (G1, G2's mutation, G5). The method questions of the same call, M23 and M24: `docs/decisions/method.md`. Ledger: E45 to E47.*

**Q130. Five public types are neither `Send` nor `Sync`.** *Found and verified.* `FacetConfig` (field `enables`), `FacetEnables` (variant `Function`), `StateFieldSpec` (field `provide`), `RangeSetUpdate` (field `filter`) and `StateCommandTarget` (field `dispatch`) each hold a callback D14 leaves without bounds. I read the signatures that take them: `Facet::define(config)` (`crates/ferrum-state/src/facet.rs:392`), `StateField::define(config)` (`:691`) and `RangeSet::update(update_spec)` (`rangeset.rs:594`) take them by value, and `StateCommand` takes its target by value for one call (`extension.rs:16`). None is held after its call: if one were, the type holding it would fail G1 itself, and G1 passes on the other 42 types, `EditorState`, `Transaction`, `Facet` and `StateField` among them. *Decision.* D6's reason is sharing what the crate keeps or returns between threads; a type that only carries an argument into one call is shared with nobody, the case D14 already decided for the callback itself. D6 now says so for every foundation crate, `design.md` §6 names the five with the member that makes each so, and G1's check accepts those five for those members only, and fails on a listed type that has become `Send` and `Sync` (a stale list). *Lost:* adding `Send` and `Sync` to the three callbacks (it forbids what D14 allows, a closure that collects into a local the caller holds, for a gain no caller needs: nothing sends a config to another thread before `define`); a check that accepts any type no function returns (constructors return `FacetConfig`, so it would need a judgment the list makes once); leaving D6 as written (G1 fails on a rule's wording while the property D6 protects holds). An `Rc` added to one of the five would pass G1 and harm nobody, since the call keeps nothing of it; the types that keep it are checked. *Confidence:* high. *Reopens:* a caller of the crates above that must build one of these on one thread and use it on another.

**Q131. The `cm6 deviation:` line of `EditorState::CHANGE_FILTER` cites `state.cm6.032`.** The item has three rows (`.d1` reproduce, `.d2` form, `.d3` deviation-a), and the deviation rests on `.d3`. `rust-crates.md` allowed an inventory id "when the decision is about that item alone", which predates rows numbered per item; a reader of a deviation needs the row that allows it, and so does G6. The rule now asks a `cm6 deviation:` line to cite its row, never an inventory id; the other qualifiers keep the old form. `crates/ferrum-state/src/state.rs:565` changes `see state.cm6.032` to `see state.cm6.032.d3`: a doc comment, no behavior; the orchestrator makes it in a commit of its own (or the implementer, if a round of findings runs first), and G6 runs again at the commit of the verdict, where every tool gate runs again anyway. *Confidence:* high.

**Q132. `EditorState.reconfigure` in the mutation list.** My list named a member cm6 does not have. What I meant is the state cm6 builds for a transaction that carries `StateEffect.reconfigure`, `StateEffect.appendConfig` or a compartment's reconfiguration: `applyTransaction` (`mirrors/cm6/state/src/state.ts:96`), which resolves a new configuration (`Configuration.resolve`, `facet.ts:469`, with `flatten`) and computes every slot in its reconfiguration mode (each slot's `reconfigure`, `facet.ts:173`, `:242`, `:337`). That is where the hazards of identity and order of U4 live (Q116 to Q119), and the first selection does not reach it: `EditorState.update` selects `resolve_transaction` and its helpers in `transaction.rs`, and nothing in `facet.rs` past the getters. I ran the tool's selection rule (its own functions, `tools/conf_check.py` `fns_with_spans`, on the tree at c08e6da, without `cargo mutants`) on the two names: they resolve, and select 40 functions (`apply_transaction`, `construct`, `Configuration::resolve`, `flatten`, `ensure_addr`, `compute_slot`, `field_slot`, `provider_slot`, the dynamic facet slot's `compute` and their helpers), about 600 lines, some already in the first run. The plan now names `applyTransaction` and `Configuration.resolve` in place of `EditorState.reconfigure`; the second run takes every mutant of the new selection not already run. The selection rule follows a call into another file only through a free or path call; that limit is now written in the plan for the verdict to state, rather than widened, because every reach across files the named items need is seeded by a name. *Confidence:* high.

**Q133. A new case a decision covers fails as a mismatch.** *What I checked.* The trial's 124: the 119 of chunk `text` call `findClusterBreak` past the end, which `state.cm6.212.d1` decides with a condition; the 4 of chunk `facet` name another field in a merge conflict, which `state.cm6.134.d2` decides without one; and `S1.F5019`, which the orchestrator took for no decided difference, is `state.cm6.188.d2`'s case: its changes replace position 1 with `a😀b` (four code units, six bytes), so every later gap's offset differs by two between the units, and the outputs differ only in one `["bound", 37]` record of the `compare` log, which is what the row says a G5 case of it looks like. So a reader sorted one case of three kinds wrongly at the first salt: sorting by reading is the hole. *Decision.* A new case is attributed to a row by a run, the way the committed lists were made (S5, second part): each row that lists fixtures gets its rule written into the harness copy of cm6 as a kept patch, which must first reproduce the row's committed list exactly; the tool runs every new case under each patch, puts the cases that change on that row's list in the scratch copy of `raw/decisions.jsonl` the fresh replay reads, and the replayer's rules apply unchanged. The two rows that are D1's arithmetic, which no patch of cm6 can write (`state.cm6.188.d2`, `state.cm6.225.d2`), are attributed by a check the tool runs from the sentence each row already has for G5; both are replaced at the improvement delta, where their cases are compared against the improved reference. A mismatch nothing attributes is a finding. Each of the five deliberate deviations of G5's sensitivity must still leave a mismatch the attribution does not absorb. *Why this and not the others.* Applying a row's condition to every new case would need a replayer change and would trust a predicate that has only been checked where the row lists fixtures (in every other fixture `condition` returns false before anything is recorded, so a predicate broader than its list was never seen); the patch is the same evidence the lists came from and checks the predicate on every new case. A list of fresh ids read by hand is what just failed. The patches are also the reference machinery the improvement delta needs right after the verdict, so they are built once. *Lost:* rows without condition given one now (188.d2's was weighed at Q100 to Q104 and rejected for a rule the delta removes). *The trial's new left-out entry*, `('state', 'char', 'state.changeByRange', 'changes.from')` in `S1.F1077` and `S1.F1119`: I read both. `S1.F1077` builds `😀é\n` and a cursor at code unit 2, and the callback deletes from `from - 1`, which is inside the astral character: D1's rule, applied by the conversion every op of the chunk shares (the approved file holds `char` for `changes.from` under `state.update`). Accepted. *Confidence:* high on attribution by run; medium on the cost of writing the patches (sixteen rows, most of them a throw at a condition), which E46 measures. *Reopens:* a patch that cannot reproduce its row's list.

**Q134. G5's coverage per entry of the shape checklist.** No fixture records which entry its draw covered, and a classifier per chunk and generator would be a reading turned into code for a count whose purpose is narrower: that the fresh cases exercise the shapes the specification's cases did, and so make "0 mismatch" mean something for each. Two counts made from data serve it. Text classes are where another position unit or text model gives another answer (`specification.md` §5, "text classes"), and the strings of a program show them: a class the committed fixtures of a chunk's seeded generators hold and its new cases lack fails. Structure is what Ferrum's branches distinguish: G2's coverage run again on the new cases alone shows which of the outcomes G2 lists as missed the new cases reach, each with a case its unit's verifier can use as the input, and how many outcomes only the committed fixtures reach. *Lost:* a tag per case written by each generator (eighteen generators changed and every committed fixture regenerated, for a label no reader needs); dropping coverage from G5 (a degenerate salt would then show only as a smaller count of new cases). *Confidence:* medium-high: the text classes are complete for D1 and D2's questions; that Ferrum's branches stand for the structural entries (adjacent, nested, overlapping, boundaries) is a deduction. *Reopens:* a fresh run whose new cases reach markedly fewer of Ferrum's outcomes than the committed ones.

## 2026-11-24 · Q135, Q136 and the U1 findings

*Decided by the consultant (Q135 and Q136, raised by the orchestrator and by the conformance verifier of U1 on 2026-11-24, and the sorting of U1's findings the orchestrator asked for). Binding text: `raw/decisions.jsonl` `state.cm6.225.d3`; `raw/improvements.jsonl` `state.imp.017`; `plan.md` "Conformance" step 1 (G5's attribution and its accepted kinds) and step 5. Ledger: E52, E53.*

**Q135. Two kinds of left-out entry of `set.update` that only G5's new cases produce.** *What I checked.* The replayer reads `filterFrom` and `filterTo` through `doc.pos` of the set's own document (`crates/ferrum-state/tests/replay/rangeset/sets.rs:406-408`), the function and the document through which it reads `add`, whose kind `('rangeset', 'char', 'set.update', 'add')` the approved file holds. I then re-derived all 44 entries from their programs, outside the replayer: for each, the document the mapped set refers to, built from the program's `changes.of` (filler for the start document, the inserted text, a line break counting one), and whether the value falls between the two halves of an astral character. 44 of 44 do (the one my first pass missed, `S47.F5069`, inserts `\r\n`, which is one break). The orchestrator's reading of `S10.F5013` named the insertion at its old position 8; in the new document `a😀b` starts at 10 and 12 is inside `😀`. *Decision.* Both kinds are D1's rule applied by the conversion every position of the chunk shares, and are accepted. The G5 check stays strict: an accepted kind is a line of `conformance/g5/accepted-kinds.jsonl` with the decision that accepted it (this one and Q133's), the tool prints each one's count and fails on a listed kind the run no longer produces, so the list cannot hide a kind nobody read. *Lost:* adding the kinds to `fixtures/no-counterpart.jsonl` (that file is checked equal to what the committed fixtures leave out, and must stay so); accepting any new `char` kind whose op already has one (the right document is what makes `char` true, and it is checked per argument, as here). *Confidence:* high.

**Q136. A clipped skip of a reverse partial cursor.** *What I checked.* `PartialTextCursor.next` clips a skip by the forward bounds whatever the direction (`mirrors/cm6/state/src/text.ts:508-509`), and `nextInner` raises a reverse cursor's skip by `pos - to` (`:496`), which after the first `next` is non-zero only when a first positive skip left the cursor above `to` (state.TextIterator.next.B8). On a reverse cursor both distances are measured on one side of the cursor and walked on the other, so, in bytes, the place reached is not the conversion of cm6's, the same kind as `state.cm6.225.d2` (Q117) and `state.cm6.188.d2`. To find the condition, not only the instance, I ran the verifier's model of cm6's cursors (the flat cursors the header of `oracle/cm6/text/interp.ts` states, in UTF-16 units and in bytes, with each skip converted as the replayer does) on 800,000 random cases over seven documents, ASCII and not: every difference falls in a step that is either a negative skip before the first `next` (`d2`) or a nonzero skip given to a reverse partial cursor after its first `next`; none outside. A narrower condition (the clip binds in either unit, or the cursor stands above `to`) still missed about twenty differences per 400,000 cases, so the condition is the whole class, which leaves out about 3 percent more steps than differ. Forward cursors never differ after their first `next`: their clips are measured on the side they walk. *Decision.* `state.cm6.225.d3`, reproduce, with that condition: Ferrum keeps cm6's arithmetic in bytes (`d1`), as for `d2`; the step is left out and the cursor is no counterpart after it; inside a model check the case is left out under the row. G5 attributes a case to `d2` or `d3` by a run (the case on the row's list in the scratch copy replays with no mismatch and a left-out entry naming only that row), since a step inside `check.partialCursor` shows in no trace. `state.imp.017`, a candidate for the improvement delta, clips by the cursor's direction, which returns text of the range as `iterRange`'s documentation says and removes the difference between the units; it is weighed with `state.imp.016`, which touches the same two lines. *Why reproduce now.* cm6's value here can be half a character, which no Rust string holds, so no form of Ferrum equals it; D11 keeps the arithmetic, and the delta right after the verdict is where the better rule is made against a reference that runs. No mirrored consumer passes a skip to an `iterRange` cursor. What I weighed against it: Ferrum panics where cm6 returns, which is below cm6's floor of stability (Q128); it is acceptable only because the delta comes before any crate calls this code, and the improvement row says that a decline must answer the panic. *Lost:* converting the skip so that Ferrum lands where cm6 does (no rule a caller can state, as at Q117); making the improvement now (D16). *Confidence:* high on the class (the model and the G5 case, which ran on both sides, agree); medium-high on the exact condition, which rests on the model, not on cm6 runs: a G5 case that differs first at a step outside it reopens it.

**What Q136 asks of the replayer, found while deciding it.** `double_skip` (`crates/ferrum-state/tests/replay/text/cursor.rs:202`) matches a partial cursor only, while `d2`'s sentence also covers the partial cursor inside a line cursor (`iterLines(from)` passes its skip unchanged, `mirrors/cm6/state/src/text.ts:527`), and the model checks' `steps` (`tests/replay/text/checks.rs:120`) apply no row's condition. Both are narrower than their rows, so a case they miss fails visibly, but a fixture listed under `d3` would then be compared at a step the row excludes. The round of findings makes each predicate the row's sentence, with literal tests in `tests/replay/rules.rs`, as Q75 to Q78 asked of every rule.

**U1's findings, sorted.** They wait for the round of findings after steps 3 and 4 (`plan.md` "Conformance" step 5), with those of the other units: the other verifiers read the tree at c08e6da, the sixth verifier re-runs reproductions against it, and one round with one `test-writer` card and one `implementer` card costs less than a round per unit. What each becomes:
1. *The 20 surviving mutants that are check gaps* (the shape and node lengths of `slice`, `replace` and `append` results, child sharing, and `len()` after a replacement on the fast path of a document of a few hundred lines or more, `src/text.rs:628`, which no fixture or G5 case caught). Their values are cm6's, so they come from runs, not from a test written by hand: a seeded generator of chunk `text` (the orchestrator's, as the generator changes of M22) that builds node documents of the sizes the verifier's probe used (`.scratch/verify-text/mprobe`) and records `length`, `lines`, the shape of `children` with each node's length (the interpreter's `shape`) and, through `same`, which children are shared with the original. Behavior rows `state.cm6.230`, `.234`, `.236`, `.244` and the new fixtures name each other both ways. The 20 mutants are then run again on those lines: each must be caught (E52). G2's line and branch coverage could not show this gap, because the code ran and only its result went unread; and G5 could not, because its generators are the committed ones with other seeds. A size class (documents deep enough to reach a structure's thresholds) belongs beside the text classes when `ferrum-view`'s plan is written.
2. *The 6 equivalent mutants:* accepted on the verifier's runs; the sixth verifier samples them with the other verdicts.
3. *The sentinel, `src/text.rs:1051-1052`:* a code finding, low. `iter_range` with a bound above `isize::MAX` must give cm6's result for that bound; the `implementer` chooses how, changing no other result; the `test-writer` writes the test from the verifier's input, with the expected value from a run of cm6 (`iterRange(0, 2 ** 63)`), not from the deduction.
4. *`state.cm6.225`, `state.cm6.240` and `g5:text:S7.F7168`:* written again by the rest of U1's verification after this decision, replacing their lines. `state.cm6.230`, `.234`, `.236` and `.244` stay `UNVERIFIED` until the round's generator and mutation run settle them.

## 2026-11-26 · Q137 to Q140: the verdict of a hazard Ferrum excludes, the integer domain, and deep layers

*Decided by the consultant (Q137 to Q140 of `ferrum-state`, raised at step 3 of the conformance stage by the orchestrator and the verifiers of U2 and U3). Binding text: `.claude/rules/decisions.md` D11 (a) widened and D17 (new; record in `docs/decisions/foundation.md`, same date); rows `state.cm6.160.d1` and `state.cm6.187.d1` (amended), `state.cm6.181.d1`, `state.cm6.189.d2`, `state.cm6.190.d2` (new) in `raw/decisions.jsonl`; `design.md` §2 (the end sentinel in `eq` and `spans`); `cards/verifier.md` items 3 and 7 and "What you write"; `plan.md` "Conformance" step 3 (the tool change and the relabelling before U4a's hazards run) and step 5.*

**Q137. A hazard that no input of Ferrum's types reaches.** *What the lines hold.* I tallied the 94 `UNREACHABLE` hazard lines of U1 to U3 by what their evidence cites: 89 cite a decision (D12 with a type, D1, D11 b through `state.cm6.229.d1` or `design.md` §10 on identity, D13 on the JavaScript error class, a row whose constructor refuses the state); five cite none. Read one by one, the five are not one kind either: `state.Line.text.B1:h1` is unobservable in cm6 itself (a JavaScript string is immutable); `state.Line.length.B2:h1` gives TypeScript's `readonly` as its reason, which is erased at run time, so a JavaScript caller can write `line.to` (the behavior row says so, F0822), and the case is excluded in Ferrum because `Line`'s fields are `pub(crate)` (`src/text.rs:808-813`), by D11 b; `state.EditorSelection.map.B6:h2` needs a selection with no range, which cm6's `fromJSON` gives (`{ranges: [], main: -1}` passes its check at `mirrors/cm6/state/src/selection.ts:176`) and Ferrum's refuses (`state.cm6.160.d1`); `state.RangeSet^compare.B11:h1` is the aliasing `state.cm6.229.d1` covers; `state.Range.B1:h1` is the visibility of `Range.create`, which gives the same `Range` as `RangeValue.range` (`rangeset.ts:52-55`), so no caller observes which was called.

*Decision.* A hazard line has four verdicts: `EXERCISED`, `FINDING`, `EXCLUDED` (no input a caller can write in Ferrum reaches it, by a decision the evidence names, with the form that excludes it) and `UNOBSERVABLE` (no caller of cm6 observes it either, with cm6's code). `UNREACHABLE` stays a verdict of branch outcomes and lines. A hazard partly excluded gets the verdict of the part a caller can write. The 89 lines are relabelled `EXCLUDED` by the rule a tool applies (their evidence cites a decision); the five get: `state.Line.text.B1:h1` `UNOBSERVABLE`; `state.Line.length.B2:h1` `EXCLUDED`, evidence "mutation of a Line after hand-over (D11 b; behavior text, F0822); Line's fields are pub(crate), src/text.rs:808-813, so no caller writes them; `readonly` is erased at run time"; `state.EditorSelection.map.B6:h2` `EXCLUDED`, evidence its own plus "cm6's fromJSON gives a selection with no range for `{ranges: [], main: -1}` (selection.ts:176); Ferrum's refuses it (state.cm6.160.d1)"; `state.RangeSet^compare.B11:h1` `EXCLUDED`, its evidence plus "state.cm6.229.d1"; `state.Range.B1:h1` `UNOBSERVABLE`, evidence "Range.create and RangeValue.range give the same Range (rangeset.ts:44-55); which one a caller called shows in nothing it reads".

*Why two verdicts and not one.* Their readers check different things: an `EXCLUDED` line is right only if the decision it cites exists and does exclude the case, which a tool can check for the first half and the verdict's list of what is not ported depends on; an `UNOBSERVABLE` line is a claim about cm6 that only its code shows. Folded into `UNREACHABLE`, a decision's consequence would read like a fact of the code, and one of the five lines shows the error the distinction exposes: a reason erased at run time. *Lost:* keeping `UNREACHABLE` with a reason (nothing checkable); `EXERCISED` with the decision as the "check" (a decision runs nothing, and G7 counts checks).

**Q138 and Q140, with U1's `state.cm6.240`: a `usize` beyond the signed range, and a sum beyond any document.** *What the findings are.* One shape in three units, which I sorted by where the value comes from:
- *A bound the caller passes that cm6 only compares.* `RangeSet`'s query bounds (`between`, `update`'s filter range, `eq`, `iter`, `iter_sets`, `goto`, `min_point_size`), the range of a change filter (`src/change.rs:643`), `Text::iter_range`'s end (`src/text.rs:1051-1052`). cm6 compares these and gives a result for any number; I ran `between` with `to` = 9.3e18 on cm6 6.7.6 and it returns as for 100. Ferrum casts them `as i64` or `as isize`, so `usize::MAX`, the way a Rust caller writes "to the end", wraps negative and the answer is wrong with no sign: a change filter that blocks nothing, `RangeSet::eq` true for sets that differ. That is the worst kind of error, and nothing in the domain row was meant to allow it: `state.cm6.187.d1` bounds what a set stores, because a stored position at 1e9 meets cm6's end sentinel; a query bound meets nothing.
- *A total of in-domain numbers read from outside data* (Q138): 1,025 sections of 2^53 - 1 read by `ChangeDesc::from_json`, whose sum overflows; outside data must not panic (D13), and a debug build does.
- *A length built in memory.* The same overflow from values the program holds, which only sharing can produce (a `Text` appended to itself 63 times keeps a tree of 63 nodes and a length of 2^63); no document is that long.
- *A position cm6 checks against the document* (`ChangeSet::of` missing its range panic past `isize::MAX`): D13 already says it panics at cm6's condition for every value, so those findings stand as they are.

*Decision.* D17, for every crate: a bound cm6 only compares takes every `usize` and gives cm6's result for that number; every held length and position is at most `isize::MAX`, a construction in memory that would exceed it panics there, and a reader of outside data returns `Err` for a total beyond it; arithmetic on held values inside the bound needs no other check. For this crate: `state.cm6.187.d1` now says its domain is stored and mapped positions and that query bounds follow D17; `state.cm6.160.d1` now covers the totals of `ChangeDesc::from_json` and `ChangeSet::from_json`. The findings of the first kind stand, the three high ones of `rangeset.rs` (`:610`, `:738`, `:830`) and `change.rs:643` among them, and the items whose lines were `CONFORMANT` pending Q140 (`state.cm6.183`, `.185`, `.189`, `.196`) are read as `NONCONFORMANT` at step 5 until the round fixes them; `state.cm6.149` and `.150` (`length`, `new_length`) likewise, for the missing check of the reader. A sentinel on a held value inside the bound is `SAFE` by D17, and the construction sites are where the check belongs (the card says so, for U4a's and U4b's lists).

*What cm6 does not do for such a bound.* `RangeSet.eq` and `RangeSet.spans` with `to` at or above 1e9 never return in cm6 (run on 6.7.6, 2026-11-26: `to` = 1e9, 2e9, 9.3e18, each stopped at 5 s; 999,999,999 returns), since an ended cursor's position is the sentinel and their loops leave only on a position above `to`; Ferrum's `spans` hangs too (the U3 verifier's probe). A hang is never parity (D11 a). Rows `state.cm6.189.d2` and `state.cm6.190.d2` give Ferrum the result cm6 gives with the ended cursor's position compared as greater than every bound, the same continuation of cm6 below 1e9 that `state.cm6.032.d3` made with `Infinity`; the round writes that patch, runs it, and writes the tests from its values.

*Lost.* Making these bounds a precondition (a panic at `usize::MAX`): cm6 gives a result there, and the panic would fall on the most common way a Rust caller writes the call. Clamping every bound at the call sites by hand with no rule: the next crate (`ferrum-view`'s viewport, heights) meets the same shape. Bounding held lengths at 2^53 as cm6's precision does: Ferrum is exact up to `isize::MAX`, and no document reaches either.

**Q139. Deep layer recursion.** *What I ran.* The U3 verifier's probe (n nested ranges `[i, 2n - i]`) on cm6 with Node's default stack: `of` fails at 10,000, `size` at 5,000, and `update` at 5,000 failed in the verifier's run and succeeded in mine, so the depth at which cm6 fails is the engine's, not cm6's. With `ulimit -s unlimited` and `node --stack-size=900000`, cm6 completes every operation at 50,000. Ferrum aborts the process on a stack overflow, which no `catch_unwind` contains, so where cm6 leaves the person editing with an error the view can catch (Q128), Ferrum loses their session. n mutually overlapping ranges make n layers (each range that overlaps the previous one in a layer goes to the next), which a file of deep nesting and a decoration per nested node can reach.

*Decision.* D11 (a) now counts an exhausted JavaScript stack with a hang: Ferrum gives what cm6 gives with a stack large enough, and recursion whose depth grows with a document or with data made from it is iterative. Row `state.cm6.181.d1` applies it to `RangeSet` (`update_inner`, `map`, `RangeSetBuilder::finish_inner`, the drop of the layer chain), with a written test of 20,000 layers on a 2 MiB thread and the large-stack run as its reference. It is made in the round of step 5, since it is the floor of stability, not an improvement. *Lost:* a depth limit that panics as cm6 throws (it would fail a set that works in cm6 on a larger stack, and its limit would depend on the thread Ferrum runs on); leaving it as low (the abort is the one failure no layer above can contain).

**Confidence.** High on Q137 and on D17's rule; high on Q139's diagnosis (two runs of cm6), medium on the cost of making three recursive functions iterative, which mutation and the fixtures will check. **Reopens.** A query bound cm6 uses in arithmetic and not only in a comparison (D17's conversion would then need cm6's arithmetic); a recursion over program-built structure (extension nesting) found to be reachable from a document.

## 2026-11-26 · Q141 and Q142: a missing field taken for a stored `null`, and a state whose construction did not finish

*Decided by the consultant (Q141 and Q142, raised by U4a's hazards runs at step 3 of the conformance). Binding text: `raw/decisions.jsonl` rows `state.cm6.040.d1` and `state.cm6.100.d2`; their patches `oracle/patches/state.cm6.040.d1.patch` and `state.cm6.100.d2.patch`; `design.md` §5 (two messages) and §6; `.claude/rules/decisions.md` D11 (c) (its record: `docs/decisions/foundation.md`, same date); `plan.md` "Conformance" steps 3 and 5; `cards/verifier.md` "Per unit".*

**What I ran.** A probe on cm6 6.7.6 in the harness (a provider `compute([f], …)` whose getter counts its calls, the field in a compartment that a reconfiguration empties or fills): a field dropped while it held `null` or `undefined` keeps the old input with no second call (`[1100]` stays, 1 call); dropped while it held `0` or `7`, the getter runs (2 calls, `[-2]`); added while its first value is `null`, the old input computed when the field was missing is kept (`[-1]` stays). Under the patch of `state.cm6.040.d1` all five call the getter. A second probe: a field's `create` keeps the state it is given, a later field's `create` throws, the caller catches it and runs `kept.update({}).state`: cm6 goes on (the first field's `update` receives its value, the second's would receive `null`); under the patch of `state.cm6.100.d2` it throws the row's message. `oracle_patch.py check` on both rows: 0 committed fixtures change under either patch, the lists are empty and equal.

**Q141.** *What cm6 does and why.* `facet.ts:179` compares a field dependency's value in the old and the new state with `==`, and `field(dep, false)` gives `undefined` for a missing field (`state.ts:68-75`). `==` exists there to compare two values of one field; that it equates a stored `null` with a missing field is JavaScript's rule, not a choice: for a non-null value the same code calls the getter, and when the field is added the kept input says the field is missing while the state has it. *Why Ferrum cannot reproduce it.* A field's value is a `T` of the caller's type; Ferrum has no value of `T` it could take for "no field", so the only way to give cm6's result would be a new member of the field's spec saying which values count as `null`, API beyond the specification that serves no caller. D12's rule that a `null` cm6 tests as absent is the absent value maps a value to `None` where the Rust type has a `None` (a parameter); a stored field value has none. *Decision.* A deviation of class (c): the dependency has changed when the field is present in one state and missing from the other, whatever its value; Ferrum's code already does this (`facet.rs:1438-1442`). D11 (c) now names the case, so that the next such comparison is classed without a question. *For the person editing and the application.* Ferrum's provider never holds an input computed from a field the state does not have; cm6's does, in exactly the cases where the field held `null` or `undefined`. *What the round adds* (`plan.md` step 5): fixtures recorded from cm6 for the four cases of the probe, the patch listing exactly the first three, a written test of `state.Facet.compute.B8` with the patched values. *Severity of the finding* `state.Facet.compute.B8:h1`: low; it becomes the row's check. Item `state.cm6.040`: `DECIDED DEVIATION` once that check exists.

**Q142.** *What is reachable.* `create` and `update` get the state being built; `EditorState` is `Clone`, so a callback can keep it. After the build panics (D13 lets a caller catch it, and `ferrum-view` will catch at entry points), or during the build (a `create` that calls `update` on its state and reads `state()`), a transaction can start from a state whose slots are not all computed. cm6 copies or reads the `null` it holds for each such slot (`state.ts:117`, `:121`, `:252`; `facet.ts:176`) and hands it to the field's `update` (`facet.ts:330-332`) or keeps it as a provider's old input. Ferrum stops at an `unreachable!` whose message says an internal invariant broke (`facet.rs:1301`, `:1342`). *Why not reproduce.* The same as Q141: a field's `update` takes a `T`, and there is no `T` for "never computed"; this is the `null` cm6 holds for a value it has not computed, which D11 (c) now names. *Why a panic, and where.* The condition is one a caller can know (it started from a state a callback kept), so by D13 it is a panic with Ferrum's own message, at the first point that would read an uncomputed start value: `Transaction::state`, before anything is copied. A panic can later become a fallible variant without breaking callers; the reverse cannot. *Reads.* Reads of a kept state compute its slots on demand, on the building thread, as cm6's do, and after a build that panicked they go on doing so: cm6 gives a result there and Ferrum can give the same. *Threads, which nobody asked.* D6 and `design.md` §6 said a state under construction is visible only on the building thread; a kept clone sent to another thread makes that false, and two threads computing one slot would run a user function twice or report a cycle that is not one. Since §6 promises it, Ferrum checks it: a slot not yet computed, read on another thread, panics with its own message (`design.md` §5, §6). cm6 has no counterpart (one thread). *Severity* of the panic lines `src/facet.rs:1301` and `:1342`: low (safe code, no unsoundness, no mirrored consumer keeps a state under construction); the round makes the code, and step 5 judges those lines again. Item `state.cm6.046` (StateField): its own behaviors are not what changed; its verdict follows its other lines, with the two panic lines settled by the round.

**For U4b's hazards runs.** `state.rs` builds the state Q142 is about: its hazards and its `shared` entries for a state's construction are judged against `design.md` §6 and `state.cm6.100.d2`; since the code at c08e6da lacks the two panics, a line that turns on them is a `FINDING` citing them, not a question. A `==` of cm6 between a stored value and a missing lookup is D11 (c): a difference with no row is a `FINDING` naming it. I found no other such comparison in `mirrors/cm6/state/src/` (`transaction.ts:81` tests `undefined` first; `transaction.ts:406` is D12's absent value).

**Lost.** *Reproduce Q141 by a spec member that marks null-like values* (API for an accident of `==`). *Leave Q142's `unreachable!`* (an internal message for a caller's misuse, which D13 forbids, and a panic that names nothing a caller can fix). *Give a transaction from an unfinished state values cm6 does not* (compute the missing start values first): no reference runs for it, and it could recurse into the slot being built. *Forbid keeping the state* (not expressible: the callback gets `&EditorState`, and `Clone` is part of D6).

**Confidence.** High on Q141 (run on cm6; the rule matches Ferrum's code). Medium-high on Q142: the panic is the conservative contract and can be relaxed; the thread check is a promise of §6 made true, with a cost of one comparison where a slot is computed. **Reopens.** A mirrored consumer of the crates above that drops a field while keeping a provider that reads it, and relies on the kept input; a surface or plugin that must start a transaction from a state a callback kept.

## 2026-11-26 · Q143, and the item verdicts

*Decided by the consultant (Q143, raised by the orchestrator from U4b's hazards run H2; with M30, `docs/decisions/method.md`). Binding text: `.claude/rules/rust-crates.md` "Annotations"; `docs/workflow/conformance.md` G6; `docs/workflow/implementation.md` §7; `tools/conf_check.py` `g6`; `docs/parity/ferrum-state/plan.md` "Conformance" steps 4 and 5. Ledger: E60, E61.*

**Question.** `raw/decisions.jsonl` has 18 rows of a deviation class; the code at c08e6da has two `/// cm6 deviation:` lines, one citing a form row (`state.cm6.134.d2`) and one an inventory id (`state.cm6.032`, Q131). H2 found three items naming their row only in doc-comment prose; H1 had accepted prose for `state.cm6.016` as the annotation. Is it?

**Decision.** No. The annotation is the qualifier line, and every row of a `deviation-*` class has it on each public item whose call shows the difference, at least one. Without it the difference is not explained (`conformance.md` §0: a decision allows it *and* the code annotates it), so the item is `NONCONFORMANT`, low, until the line exists. G6 now joins both ways: I added the second direction to `conf_check g6` and ran it at 658d643: 18 of 18 rows are cited by no line (032.d3's line cites the inventory id). The round of step 5 writes the 18 lines (the rows whose code the round writes get theirs with it), and G6 must pass both ways at the verdict's commit. For later crates the hand-off's annotation scan checks it, and each unit's code step its own rows, so the gap cannot reach conformance again.

**Why.** The line has a fixed form because its readers find differences by searching one form and joining it to the rows: G6, the implementers of `ferrum-view` and the crates above, a developer who knows cm6 and wants to know where Ferrum is not cm6. Prose that names a row is found by none of them; and the gate passed with every one of 18 deviations unannotated, because it checked only that each line cites a row. That is a hole in a gate, not a matter of verifier style, and the class is closed by the tool for every row, not by re-judging the three H2 found. The rows of class `form` are left as they are: I read the 22: 21 give a call, a type or a check its Rust form (inputs a type excludes, two functions for an overload, a behavior checked in another package), which a caller sees in the signature, and none changes what a call Ferrum accepts returns; the one that does, 134.d2 (which of several conflicts the panic names), has its line, and I read each `form` row's lines against its text in step 5's review of explained differences. A rule by row class keeps the check mechanical; a rule by "observable" would need a reading of each row every time.

**The item verdicts.** H1 and H2's item lines stand as written: the final verdicts are not theirs to fix one by one. An item's verdict is defined by data (its behaviors' lines, its rows, the gates and now the annotation join), so a tool derives every sampled item's verdict and prints each recorded `item` line that disagrees and each finding it cannot tie to an item; step 5's verdicts are its output after the round (plan step 4, "The items tool"). This also takes the item lines out of step 4's sample (M30).

**Lost.** *Accept prose that names the row* (no reader can find it, and the join cannot check it). *Require a line for every row of every class* (21 lines that only repeat a signature). *Re-judge the item lines of H1 and H2 by hand* (a reading of what the tool derives, done twice).

**Confidence.** High. **Reopens.** A deviation row whose difference shows on no public item (its line would have nowhere to go), or a `form` row whose result a valid call observes without a line.

## 2026-11-27 · Step 4 classified, Q144, Q145, E60 and the round of step 5

*Decided by the consultant (the classification and the round asked for by the orchestrator at the end of step 4; Q144 raised by the orchestrator, Q145 by the rechecker of U1-a2, both 2026-11-27). Method changes: `docs/decisions/method.md`, "M33". Ledger: E58, E60, E62 (results), E63 to E66.*

**The rows that were not `agree`.** 203 rows, of which the re-checkers wrote 188 `agree`, 11 `overturn` and 4 `later decision`. I read each one against its line, its sources and the decisions in force, and against the re-checker's re-run. After that reading: 183 `agree`, 9 `overturn` (1 high), 11 `later decision`. What changed and why:
- *Overturns that stand, all low but one.* `conformance-change.jsonl:74` (hazard `ChangeDesc.length.B1:h1`): a label error.
